# THE TOMB
# OF SEMIRAMIS
## *Hermetically sealed,*
### *Which*
## IF A WISE MAN OPENS IT,
### *And not*
## CYRUS, THE AMBITIOUS AND AVARICIOUS,
## *That king will find inexhaustible treasures of wealth,*
## *sufficient for his needs.*
<note>This refers to a legendary story where Queen Semiramis (or Nitocris) left an inscription on her tomb claiming that any king of Babylon who lacked money could find it within. When Cyrus the Great opened it, he found no money, but only a message rebuking his greed. Here, it is used as an alchemical metaphor: only the "wise" seeker, not the greedy one, can find the true treasure.</note>

->Give me one who loves, and he feels what I say; but if I speak to one who is cold,<-
->he does not feel what I say. <note>original: *D. Aug. d. amor. div.*</note> St. Augustine, *On Divine Love*.<-

->To the most Noble and Distinguished Men, the Lords of the<-
->Holy Roman Empire’s Academy of the <term>Curious in Nature</term>,<-
->And<-
->MOST HONORED PATRONS.<-
<note>The *Academia Naturae Curiosorum*, founded in 1652, was one of the first scientific societies in Europe, eventually becoming the Leopoldina.</note>

Not only in ancient times, but also in this old age of the world, nothing lies more deeply buried, nor has been more desired by everyone—especially by <term>lovers of the arts</term> <note>original: *Technophilis*</note>—than the Science of the Wisdom of that great mystery, which is called by the well-known name: the *Philosophers' Stone*. For this reason, I have presented this science as if buried and shadowed under the form of the *Tomb of Semiramis*, the once most wise Queen of Babylon, while preserving the inscription of that same monument...

...description, we draw out from the tomb and present to the literary world to be viewed and opened; *Which if a Wise man, and not Cyrus—the ambitious and greedy—opens, he will find the inexhaustible treasures of the riches of Kings, as much as suffices.* <note>The author continues the allegory from the title page: alchemical knowledge is a "treasure" that remains hidden from those motivated only by greed, like the Persian King Cyrus in the legend.</note> I add to this constant health; these you might call the two main pillars of a happier life.

But perhaps there will be "Sages" on high to whom opening this Tomb will seem like women's work or child's play. I would have them know, however, that this science is *a very deep river, in which the Lamb walks*—meaning the simple person makes progress—*and the Elephant swims*—meaning the most learned men drift among their own opinions, errors, and doubts, and are even submerged. <note>original: *Amnem altissimum, in quo Agnus ambulat... & Elephas natat.* This is a famous medieval proverb, often applied to the Bible, suggesting it is shallow enough for a lamb to cross safely but deep enough for an elephant to drown.</note> While one man is still doubtful about the true subject of the <term>Great Work</term> <note>The *Magnum Opus*, the process of creating the Philosophers' Stone.</note> (and is indeed entirely off-track), another is laboriously anxious about acquiring <term>Sophic Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurio sophico*. Not common quicksilver, but a specialized alchemical substance believed to be the "seed" of metals.</note> and exhausts himself in vain labors, knowing neither what it is nor what form it takes. Another man torments himself with empty sorrow over the nature of <term>Philosophic Fire</term>, the magical elements, the key, or the dissolving <term>menstruum</term> <note>An alchemical solvent.</note>—asking from where it should be drawn, whether it is sweet or corrosive, cold or fiery. I have often groaned over the things such men's hands have produced and the fruitlessness of their sweat, where in the end they find nothing but vanity and affliction of spirit. Therefore, moved by a sting of charity and pity for them, I freely share the light mercifully granted to me, so that they may find the key to the sanctuary of that sacred science—even though I may be accused by many of being guilty of violating the vow of silence.

Indeed, your own curious industry and tireless interest, most Excellent Men, regarding the curious secrets and works of <term>many-skilled Nature</term> <note>original: *Polydædalæ Naturæ*. A reference to Daedalus, the mythical craftsman, implying nature is a complex, artistic builder.</note>—which has invited the whole literary world to share its more hidden things through your sweet allure—has also driven me to make you, the true sons of all-encompassing science, the heirs of this most curious work of all nature which I possess.

As for who I am, do not ask. I am a man who strives to benefit others; your friend, and an admirer of your virtues, known to many, at least by name.

Farewell then, you who are Curious of Nature and <term>Initiates of the Art</term> <note>original: *Artis Mystæ*. 'Art' here refers specifically to Alchemy.</note>, exalted Ger-

# THE TOMB
## OF SEMIRAMIS
### HERMETICALLY SEALED
# CHAPTER I.
## Concerning the Physical Subject of the Philosophers' Stone.

*Let the beginning of our work be the FEAR OF THE LORD, and the end be CHARITY and LOVE of neighbor. Therefore, as we set about such a divine work with the Good GOD, we must first inquire: what is its subject? For just as a farmer prepares his field for the harvest in vain unless he is certain of his seed, so too does the man who prepares his chemical field for nothing if he is ignorant of what he sows into it. And on this point, many today still distress themselves and are pulled into differing opinions.*

*But it is not the place here to wish to discuss all these things: while some seek in the Animal Kingdom—in Blood, Sperm, Sweat, Urine, Hair, Dung, Eggs, Snakes, toads, spiders, etc.; others are occupied with great but empty effort for the <term>Magisterium</term> <note>The "Mastery" or the successful completion of the alchemical process.</note> in the Vegetable Kingdom, primarily in Wine. For although it is clear to us that in both Kingdoms—and indeed in Man (especially his heart) as well as in Wine—one supreme medicine for our health can be obtained; for just as gold encompasses the powers of all minerals, so do these two encompass the powers of all animals and vegetables, as if collected into one; yet, no <term>Adept</term> <note>A successful alchemist who has discovered the secret of the Stone.</note> ever dreamed that the Great Work of the wise could be made from them. It remains, therefore, that it must be sought from the Mineral Kingdom; but here too there is a great circle of those in disagreement, so that we truly have need of an Oedipus <note>A reference to Oedipus solving the riddle of the Sphinx; a metaphor for needing a master riddle-solver.</note>: for there are those who would draw it out from the "middle minerals," as they call them—namely Salt, Niter, Alum, and others of that kind...*

<summary>The author concludes his dedication and begins Chapter 1 by identifying the "subject" or raw material of the Philosophers' Stone, dismissing animal and vegetable sources in favor of the mineral kingdom.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' Stone, Animal Kingdom, Vegetable Kingdom, Mineral Kingdom, Adept, Magisterium, alchemy, 1674</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the material source of the Philosophers' Stone. The catchword "ere" from the previous page completes the word "quaerere" (to seek).</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>THE TOMB OF SEMIRAMIS</header>

...to seek <note>original: *quaerere*</note> them, but in vain; since these contain nothing of <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: *argenti vivi*; literally "living silver" or mercury, considered the "seed" of metals.</note> within them into which they might be resolved; *In which mud we also were immersed in our initial ignorance.*

Therefore, it remains established that metals are the physical subject of our blessed Stone. But here too the matter is at a crossroads: for some metals are imperfect, and others are perfect.

To briefly unburden ourselves, we say that all fused metals—and especially those not fused even if they are imperfect—can be the subject of the Stone through the innermost purification of their original stain <note>Alchemists believed base metals were "sick" or "stained" versions of gold.</note> (though this is exceedingly difficult and appears nearly impossible). Concerning this, **Flamel** <note>Nicholas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), a famous French scrivener and legendary alchemist.</note> says: *Some have worked in Jupiter <note>Tin</note>, some in Saturn <note>Lead</note>, but I, he says, worked in the Sun <note>Gold</note> and found it.* And in the *Exercises on the Turba* <note>Referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers," one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts.</note> it is read: *All metals, clean and unclean, are inwardly Sun, Moon, and Mercury, but there is only one true Sun which is drawn out from them.* And the author of the secret work *Hermetic Philosophy* <note>Jean d'Espagnet, author of *Arcanum Philosophiae Hermeticae* (1623).</note>, in canon 16, says: *Whoever seeks the art of perfecting and multiplying imperfect metals outside the nature of metals is on the path of error; for metals must be sought from metals, just as the human species is from man and the bovine from the ox.* And in canon 18 he discusses further: *Perfect bodies are endowed with a more perfect seed; therefore, under the hard crust of perfect metals lies the perfect seed, which he who knows how to extract by philosophical resolution has entered the royal road.* So also that anonymous author **Philalethes** <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a 17th-century alchemist, author of *The Open Entrance to the Closed Palace of the King*.</note> says in *The Entrance to the Sacred Palace of the King*, chapter 19, regarding progress in the first 40 days: *Doubtless there is **GOLD** in all metals, even the common ones, but it is closer in **GOLD** and **SILVER**.* Although (as the same Adept well says) there is still one thing in the metallic kingdom of wonderful origin, in which our Gold is closer than in common Gold and Silver, if you seek it in the hour of its birth, which is melted in our Mercury like ice in warm water, etc.

But setting aside these more imperfect things for now, we declare those two great and more perfect luminaries, the **SUN** and the **MOON**—namely Gold and Silver—as the physical subject of the Stone. This is the path that a great part of the Philosophers has followed, and they have achieved their desires. And **Augurellus** <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), author of the alchemical poem *Chrysopoeia*.</note> declares this in book 2 of his *Chrysopoeia* when he says:

> — You shall receive a metal slightly
> and entirely purged of filth, in whose secret part
> the Spirit has receded, and pressed under the thick mass
> it lives hidden, and waits to be loosed from its bonds
> and the prison of heaven, poured out into thin borders.

<meta>catchword: THE SAME (IDEM)</meta>

## *The SAME author in the book Gold-Making* <note>original: *IDEM in Chrysop. l.* referring to the author of the *Chrysopoeia*.</note>

->age<- <note>This fragment likely completes a word from the previous page's layout.</note>

> Now you may seek the beginnings of gold from elsewhere,
> [But] in gold are the seeds of gold, although they recede,
> Hidden deep, and must be sought by us with long labor.

<margin>*LULLIUS, Codicillus p. 28*</margin>
And concerning the dignity of both luminaries <note>The "luminaries" are the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver).</note>, that star of **Spagyric Philosophy** <term>Spagyric</term> <note>From the Greek words for "to separate" and "to combine"; a term for alchemical medicine-making popularized by Paracelsus.</note> [says]: For this reason, two metals are found to be more purified than others, namely, Gold and Silver, without which this art cannot be completed. Since in them is the purest substance of <term>sulphur</term> <note>In alchemy, "Sulphur" refers to the active, masculine principle of combustion and soul, not just the yellow chemical element.</note> perfectly purified by the ingenuity of nature, our medicine can be drawn from those two bodies prepared with their own sulphur or arsenic, and without them it cannot be had. And the blast of the **TRUMPET** <note>original: *BUCCINÆ*; referring to the alchemical text *Clangor Buccinae* (The Sound of the Trumpet).</note> [declares]: You must work prudently and skillfully, because without <term>ferment</term> <note>A substance, like yeast in bread, that communicates its nature to the whole mass.</note> there can be neither Sun nor Moon; and no other seed or ferment is appropriate and useful, except Gold for the Red <note>The *Rubedo*, or final stage of the alchemical work producing the red stone for transmuting gold.</note> and Silver for the White <note>The *Albedo*, or white stage, for transmuting silver.</note>. These bodies, having first been refined under weight, must then be sown so that they may putrefy and be destroyed; for when one form is destroyed, another more noble form is put on. And this happens by the mediation of our water alone. Hence a certain **ANONYMOUS** author concludes best in his response: Just as fire is the principle of making fire, so Gold is the principle of Gold; as is the cause, so is the effect. As is the father, so is the son; as is the seed, so is the fruit. Man generates man, and a lion generates a lion.

But you will say: the Philosophers claim that the material must be such that the rich and poor can have it equally, whence it is said: GOD has granted this treasure to be sought by all men, nor does He envy any mortal such great goods, unless someone renders himself unworthy by the wicked affections of his heart. And **GEBER** <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), the father of Arabic chemistry.</note> says: it is not necessary to consume your wealth; for if you are not ignorant of the principles of the art which we hand down to you, you will reach the completed mastery at a low price. For if Gold, or anything similarly expensive, were the only [material], then the poor would be forced to set aside this glorious work; since it happens that the Craftsman often errs, a poor man could not repeat the work after an error was made—which must absolutely be done if no other remedy remains. And the *Lily* <note>Referring to the text *Lilium Intelligentiae*.</note> [says]: Moreover, this Stone is openly bought at the lowest price; if the sellers had known it, they would hold it in their hands and would in no way sell it. And another **ANONYMOUS** author: Our expenses do not exceed the price of two florins. Which **ARNOLD** <note>Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a famous physician and alchemist.</note> confirms: Hold firmly that the expenses of our most noble Art do not exceed the price of two gold pieces in its purchase, that is, its operation. And

<summary>This page argues that while Gold and Silver are the essential "seeds" or "ferments" for alchemy, the actual starting material (the Stone) is inexpensive and accessible to all, preventing the art from being restricted only to the wealthy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the materials of the Great Work, specifically addressing the paradox that while the "seeds" of the work are found in gold and silver, the common forms of these metals are considered "dead." It transitions into Chapter II, which defines "Philosophical Gold."</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>THE TOMB OF SEMIRAMIS <note>original: *TUMBA SEMIRAMIDIS*. This title refers to a famous alchemical allegory where an inscription on a tomb promises wealth to whoever opens it, only to find a warning against greed inside.</note></header>

**GEBER** <note>The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, though here likely referring to "Pseudo-Geber," the influential 13th-century author of the *Summa Perfectionis*.</note>: If in the course of the work you lose your money <note>original: *æs*; literally "bronze" or "copper," but used here to mean "capital" or "materials."</note>, do not unjustly criticize us, but blame your own imprudence: for our science does not require great expenses.

*To which we respond: We have never denied that, besides Gold and Silver, there is also another subject of lesser price: wherein we have not excluded the imperfect metals, just as we recalled from* **ANONYMOUS PHILALETHA** <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist.</note> *with these words: "There is still one thing in the metallic kingdom of wonderful origin," etc. Although many of the Philosophers wish this "vile price" to be understood as referring to our dissolving solvent <note>original: *menstruo nostro solvente*.</note>.*

*You will say further from* **SENDIVOGIUS** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a Polish alchemist.</note> *in treatise XI: "In your labors do not accept the gold and silver of the common people: for these are dead."*

*We respond: by conceding that the Stone is not made from the gold and silver of the common people, as such, and as long as they remain dead; But once they have been resurrected, and reduced back into their first seminal nature, they are made similar to the Gold of the Philosophers, so that they not only give forth seed from themselves, but also serve in the place of* <term>ferment</term> <note>A substance that acts like yeast, "leavening" the alchemical base and transforming it into gold.</note>. *Which the Philosopher confirms in these words: "Those of the present and the past have not made anything except Gold from Gold, and Silver from Silver, yet that Gold or Silver was not that of the common people."*

*From which it is clear, that the Gold of the Philosophers is not the gold of the common people, neither in color, nor in substance, but that which is extracted from them, which is the white and red <term>tincture</term> <note>The "tincture" is the concentrated essence or "medicine" of the Stone that can color or transform metals.</note>.*

---

## C A P U T  II.
### What is Physical or Philosophical Gold?

Philosophical Gold or Silver is a metallic body resolved into its ultimate matter, which is the first matter of the Stone (*namely, into Mercury*), and it is proven: Every thing is made from that into which it can be resolved, but all metals are reduced into <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: *argenteitatem vivam*; literally "living silver-ness" or mercury.</note>, therefore they were originally Mercury.

For it is (*according to the common opinion of the Philosophers*) in Mercury that the wise seek. ALSO: Mercury is the root in **Alchemy**, since from it, through it, and in it are all metals. And THEOPHRASTUS <note>Referring to Paracelsus, whose full name was Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim.</note>, that most profound sea of **Spagyric Philosophy**, speaks thus concerning the first matter of metals: To extract Mercury from metallic bodies is nothing other than to resolve them, or to reduce them into their first matter, that is, running Mercury, such as it was in the center of the earth before the generation of metals—namely, a moist and viscous vapor

<meta>catchword: (which</meta>

<summary>This page explains that while common gold and silver are "dead," they contain the "seeds" which, when resurrected into "Philosophical Gold," act as the ferment for the Great Work. It defines Philosophical Gold as a metallic body returned to its primary state: a moist, viscous, mercurial vapor.</summary>
<keywords>Geber, Philaletha, Sendivogius, Paracelsus, Philosophical Gold, Mercury, Quicksilver, Ferment, First Matter, Tincture</keywords>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>THE TOMB OF SEMIRAMIS.</header>

(which is the Gold or Silver of the Philosophers) containing within itself, invisibly, Mercury and Sulfur, the natural principles of all metals. Such a Mercury possesses ineffable virtues and powers, and contains divine secrets.

# CHAPTER III.
## *On the Preparation of bodies for the Mercury of the Philosophers.*

**AVICENNA** <note>The Latinized name of Ibn Sina (c. 980–1037), a Persian polymath whose works were central to medieval alchemy.</note> says: If you wish to labor, it is necessary that you begin your work in the solution or <term>sublimation</term> <note>the process of heating a substance into vapor and then cooling it back into a solid to purify it</note> of the two luminaries <note>original: *duorum luminarium*; referring to the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver)</note>. Indeed, the first step of the work is that <term>Quicksilver</term> <note>original: *Argentum vivum*; Mercury</note> may be made from them. But since these bodies—being more perfect—are more tightly bound and have attained a harder coagulation, they first require preparation and "physical" <term>calcination</term> <note>reducing a metal to a fine powder or "ash" through heat or chemical corrosion</note> so that they can be reduced back into Mercury. This is not quite as necessary for Silver [for because of its purity and softness, our water acts easily upon it], which is not the case for Gold and the other metals; all of these require calcination, upon which our water then acts more easily, especially if any impurities have been purged, due to the similarity of the substance.

*On the calcination of bodies from the secret work on the Philosopher’s Stone by the Lord Doctor and Bishop of Trent:* <note>Likely referring to Johannes de Altasierra or another ecclesiastical figure associated with alchemical traditions.</note> Metals to be dissolved in <term>virgin’s milk</term> <note>original: *lacte virginis*; a symbolic term for a potent, often white-colored solvent or corrosive water</note> must first be calcined or purified. Let the finest <term>Luna</term> <note>Silver</note>, finely filed, be dissolved in <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: *aqua forti*; literally "strong water," or nitric acid</note> and distilled rainwater in which <term>ammoniac</term> <note>sal ammoniac</note> or common salt has been dissolved. Let it be precipitated into a white <term>calx</term> <note>a fine metallic powder</note>. Once the water is poured off, let the calx be washed and "sweetened" with other warm rainwater so that all saltiness and sharpness are removed; let it be dried afterward, and it will be a most pure calx.

*But let Gold be calcined in this manner:* Create an <term>amalgam</term> <note>a mixture of a metal with mercury</note> with Gold (which should first be purified by the <term>Black Eagle</term> <note>original: *Aquilam nigram*; an alchemical term usually referring to sal ammoniac or a specific volatile reagent used to "open" the body of gold</note> so that it becomes exceedingly beautiful and glorious) and Mercury, which has been perfectly purified by salt and vinegar and squeezed through leather. Place this in purified aqua fortis so that all the Mercury is dissolved; pour the aqua fortis off from the calx of the <term>Sun</term> <note>Gold</note>, wash the calx with warm water as described above, and dry it with gentle heat. If this calx is skillfully and lightly <term>reverberated</term> <note>heated in a furnace where the flame is reflected onto the material without direct contact</note>, yet without melting, it will turn into a most beautiful <term>crocus</term> <note>original: *crocum*; literally "saffron," referring to a deep orange or red metallic oxide powder</note>.

*Gold is otherwise calcined in this way, so it can be reduced into the primary matter or the Mercury of the Philosophers, about which* **PARACELSUS** <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and occultist.</note> *says in Book 7 of the Metamorphoses on Resuscitation:* namely, let the metal be calcined with revivified Mercury, by placing the Mercury with the metal in a <term>sublimatorium</term> <note>a vessel used for sublimation</note>, digesting them together until it becomes

<summary>This page describes the transition from alchemical theory to laboratory practice, focusing on "calcination"—the process of breaking down gold and silver into a fine powder so they can be returned to their primal, mercurial state. It provides specific chemical recipes involving nitric acid, salts, and heating techniques attributed to authorities like Avicenna and Paracelsus.</summary>

...let an <term>amalgam</term> <note>a mixture of mercury with another metal, usually gold or silver</note> be made. From there, <term>sublimate</term> <note>purify by heating into vapor then cooling back to solid</note> the Mercury with a modest fire, and grind it again with the metallic lime; repeat the digestion and sublimation as before, and do this as many times as necessary until the lime, when moved toward a burning candle, flows like ice or wax. Place this metal, thus prepared, to <term>digest</term> <note>to subject to steady, gentle heat over a long period</note> in a horse’s belly <note>original: *ventrem equinum*; a common alchemical term for a heap of fermenting horse manure, used as a natural heat source</note>, or in a <term>B. M.</term> <note>original: *Balneum Mariae*; "Mary’s Bath" or a water bath</note> at a decent heat, digesting it for a month; then the metal will be converted into <term>living Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius vivens*</note> (that is), into the <term>first matter</term>, which is called the **Mercury of the Philosophers** and the **Mercury of the metals**, which many have sought, but few have found.

**JOACHIM POLEMANN** <note>Joachim Polemann (fl. 1650s) was a chemist and follower of Van Helmont, known for his work *Novum Lumen Medicum* regarding the "Mystery of Sulfur."</note>, in his work on the mystery of the Sulfur of the Philosophers, by the benefit of his doubled and saturated corrosive, divides the metal into the smallest atoms and tears it apart, so that it may be handed over to the fiery dissolving <term>menstruum</term> <note>a solvent used to extract a specific essence or "soul" from a substance</note> to extract the **tinting soul**.

Another and better way is used by us for calcination, which we prefer to call the *first solution*; and this is done if the aforementioned limes of gold or silver, placed in a phial, are poured over with the **wine of life** <note>original: *vinum vitae*</note>, which is our <term>menstruum</term> (of which more below in Chapter 6) to the height of one finger’s breadth. With a <term>capitellum</term> <note>the "head" or top of a distilling apparatus, also called an alembic</note> or alembic attached, let them digest in ash or even in sand, and let them be <term>coagulated</term> <note>thickened or solidified from a liquid state</note>. Once coagulated, pour on new menstruum as above and coagulate again, and do this a third and fourth time, or until the metallic lime melts in light like wax or ice, which is the sign of sufficient **Philosophical calcination**; and this is done with the preservation of the metal in its first virtue. And this is what **ARISTOTLE** <note>Referring to "Pseudo-Aristotle," the name attached to many medieval alchemical texts like the *Rosarium Philosophorum*.</note> says in the *Rosary*: *Join your son Gabritius, who is dearer to you than all your sons, with his sister Beja, who is a shining, sweet, and tender girl.* <note>Gabritius (the King/Sulfur/Gold) and Beja (the Queen/Mercury/Silver) represent the union of masculine and feminine principles in the alchemical process.</note>

# CHAPTER IV.
## On the second and true Philosophical solution of bodies, and the reduction into Mercury.

Once the calcination or *first solution* has been performed, of which we spoke in the preceding chapter—and which (as the **ANONYMOUS** Philosopher says in his response in the *Golden Tractate on the Philosophers' Stone*) ought to be sweet and entirely natural (that is), which would dissolve the subject without a harsh noise <note>referring to a violent chemical reaction like bubbling or hissing</note> and with the preservation of its **radical moisture**—then the bodies thus calcined must be committed to an **H. S.** <note>original: *Hermetice Sigillata*; Hermetically Sealed</note> phial, and for a **Philosophical month** <note>typically 40 days</note> must be digested or <term>putrefied</term> in a gentle heat of a water bath or "dew" <note>original: *roris*; likely referring to a very gentle, moist heat</note>; for a voluntary solution is better than a violent one, and a temperate one is better than one that is hurried, as the Philosopher says. And thus the second and true solution of the metal into a viscous water is achieved, or into a certain oiliness with the preservation of the **radical moisture**, in which is the true **metallic sulfur**, and at the same time the true and...

...is the most noble <term>Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of liquidity and transformation, the "solvent" of the world</note>; for one always exists as the magnet of the other, and the solvent remains with the dissolved substance, desiring to stay together inseparably because of the similarity of their substance. Hence the ancients said: **"Nature rejoices in nature; nature conquers nature; nature changes nature."** <note>original: *natura naturâ gaudet, natura naturam vincit, immutat*; a fundamental alchemical axiom suggesting that only like substances can truly transform one another</note> By this, a "formal" or essential solution is distinguished from a "corrosive" or destructive solution.

It should be known, moreover, that from <term>Luna</term> <note>silver</note> a green liquor or tincture is born, which is the true **Elixir of the Moon**, the highest secret for strengthening the brain. From <term>Sol</term> <note>gold</note>, however, through an equal process of <term>putrefaction</term> <note>the stage of breaking down a substance through gentle heat, often associated with a black color</note>, a liquor of the deepest redness is produced, which is the true **Elixir of the Sun** and the <term>quinta essentia</term> <note>the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of a material</note> of the metal. Concerning this, **GEBER** <note>the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), the legendary father of Islamic and European alchemy</note> says: **"We make bloody Gold <note>original: *Aurum sanguinolentum*</note>, better than that produced by nature, which nature itself cannot make."**

GEBER speaks further of this "viscosity" in his *Summa*: **"We have tested every single thing most exactly, with proven reasoning, but we could never find anything that remains permanent in the fire except for the viscous humidity alone, which is the root of all metals. All other humidities—because they are not well-united in their internal balance—flee easily from the fire, and their elements are easily separated from one another. But viscous humidity (namely, Mercury) is never consumed in the fire, nor is its water separated from its earth; rather, they either both remain together, or they depart together."**

But you may ask: in what proportion should the <term>menstruum</term> <note>a solvent, named after the lunar cycle (menses) because of the time required for digestion</note> be "married" to the metal? The ***ROSARIUS Philosophorum*** <note>The *Rosary of the Philosophers*, a highly influential 16th-century alchemical text</note> says: **"Just as in the making of bread, a little leaven raises and ferments a great quantity of dough, so also that small amount of 'earth' is sufficient for the nourishment of the entire Stone."** **ARISTOTLE** <note>Many alchemical works were falsely attributed to the Greek philosopher Aristotle in the Middle Ages</note> specifies the weight, saying: **"Do it thus: cook it until the earth (that is, the Gold) has swallowed up ten times its weight of water."**

The **Author of the New Light** <note>referring to Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist</note> breaks out into these words at the end of his book: **"The water must be in a tenfold proportion to one part of the body, and by this way we make Mercury without the 'common mercury' of the marketplace. We do this by taking our 'Mercurial water' (that is, the mercurial oil of salt, putrefied and distilled), which is an oily vapor in a tenfold proportion to one part of the body of gold. When these are closed in a vessel through continuous cooking, the Gold becomes Mercury—that is, an oily vapor—and not the common mercury, as some falsely imagine."**

# CHAPTER V.
### What is the "Fifth Essence" properly?

**PARACELSUS** <note>The famous Swiss physician and occultist (1493–1541) who pioneered the use of chemicals in medicine</note> discourses thus in Book 3 of *On Long Life*, Chapter 2: **"The Fifth Essence is nothing other than the goodness of nature, such that the whole of nature passes into a <term>Spagyric</term> <note>the alchemical process of 'separating and recombining' to purify a substance</note> mixture and temperament, in which nothing corruptible and nothing contrary can be found."**

It cites authorities like Geber and Paracelsus to define the Quintessence as the incorruptible "goodness" of a substance.</summary>

THE SAME <note>referring to Paracelsus, the famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note> *in Book 4 of the Archidoxa <note>Paracelsus's "Great Doctrines," one of his most influential works on chemical medicine</note> says concerning the fifth essence:* The Fifth Essence <note>original: *Quinta essentia*; the purest, celestial core of a substance, distinct from the four physical elements</note> is a single matter, which is physically extracted from all living things, and from everything that possesses life; it is separated from all impurity and mortality, refined most purely, and divided from all the elements.

And a little later in the same work: You ought to know regarding the Fifth Essence that it is small and modest, dwelling within some wood, some herb, or stone, or such like: the remainder is the pure body, from which we learn the separations of the elements, etc.

RUPESCISSA <note>John of Rupescissa (c. 1310–1366), a Franciscan friar who pioneered the use of "quintessences" or alcohol-based medicines</note> *concerning the fifth essence, chapter 5, near the end;* Therefore, it is devised by a divine breath <note>original: *divino spiraculo*</note> that through continuous ascensions and descents <note>the process of circulation or reflux distillation in a glass vessel</note> the Fifth Essence, which we seek, is separated from the corruptible body of the four elements. This happens because that which is sublimated a second time, and often, is more subtle and glorified, and separated from the corruption of the four elements, than when it ascends only once; and so on up to a thousand times. Through this continuous ascent and descent, that which is sublimated reaches such a power of glorification that it becomes an incorruptible compound, almost like heaven, and made of the matter of heaven. Therefore it is called the "Fifth Essence," because it relates to our body just as heaven relates to the whole world; it functions in almost the same way that art can imitate nature, as if by a very close and natural likeness.

## CHAPTER VI.

->*Concerning the Philosophical Fire, or the Solvent Menstruum, or our Liquor Alkaest.*<- <note>The "Menstruum" is the alchemical solvent used to dissolve metals; the "Alkaest" is a legendary universal solvent capable of reducing any body to its first matter.</note>

The Philosophers have always kept the preparation of this water or most noble juice (*which is the true Bath of the King*) hidden. So much so that Count Bernard of Trevisan <note>a 15th-century Italian alchemist known for his search for the Philosopher's Stone</note> in *Book 2* said that he would expose it to no man in plain words; he had pledged a vow to GOD, to the Philosophers, and to fairness, since it is the most secret mystery of the entire work. And indeed! For if this liquor were open to anyone, then children would mock our wisdom, the foolish would be equal to the ingenious, and the whole world in its entirety would rush here with blind impulse, casting itself headlong into the depths of Acheron <note>a river in the Greek underworld; here used to mean ruin or hell</note> without any regard for fairness or piety.

AUGURELLUS <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), an Italian humanist and alchemical poet</note> indicates that this solvent <note>original: *menstruum*</note> is Mercury, when he sings thus:
> Nor you, Cyllenius <note>an epithet for Mercury/Hermes, who was born on Mount Cyllene</note>, ever in your bold undertakings...

<summary>This page defines the "Fifth Essence" as a celestial, incorruptible substance extracted through repeated distillation and introduces the "Alkaest," a secret universal solvent. It emphasizes that the preparation of this solvent must remain hidden from the unworthy to prevent social and spiritual ruin.</summary>

# THE TOMB OF SEMIRAMIS.
<meta>catchword: the fire</meta>

If the "living silver" <note>original: *argentum quod vivere dicunt*; referring to Quicksilver or Mercury</note> as the common people call it is lacking,
It is sufficient; for the first principles <note>the fundamental building blocks of matter</note> provide for such great things.

GEORGE RIPLEY <note>an influential 15th-century English alchemist</note> thinks the same in the prologue of his *Twelve Gates*: "I shall surely teach you that these are the Mercuries, which are the keys of knowledge, which RAYMOND <note>Raymond Lull, a legendary medieval philosopher to whom many alchemical works were attributed</note> calls his solvents <note>original: *menstrua*; liquids used to dissolve solids</note>, without which nothing is done." GEBER <note>the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, the father of chemistry</note> names it otherwise: "By the highest GOD, this is that water which lights lamps, illuminates houses, and offers an abundance of riches." O water of our sea! O our salt-nitre <note>original: *sal nitri*; a chemical often identified as the "universal salt"</note> turning within the sea of the world! O our vegetable! O our fixed and volatile sulfur! O the 'dead head' <note>original: *caput mortuum*; the solid residue remaining after distillation</note> or the dregs of our sea!

TRIDENSINUS <note>likely an author or a specific alchemical manuscript tradition</note> in his *Secret Work on the Philosophers' Stone* says: The philosophers called their water, which they used for completing the work, virgin’s milk, curd, morning dew, the fifth essence, the water of life, the daughter of the philosophers, and so on. PARACELSUS also names it variously: AZOTH <note>the universal medicine or first principle of metals</note>, spirit of wine <note>alcohol</note>, the tempered and circulated spirit; mercurial water; SENDIVOGIUS <note>Michael Sendivogius, a famous Polish alchemist</note> calls it "steel"; RUPESCISSA <note>John of Rupescissa, a 14th-century pioneer of medical chemistry</note> calls it "most nobly distilled vinegar." That most profound philosopher-by-fire <note>an alchemist who works through the transformative power of heat</note> VAN HELMONT <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont, a Flemish chemist who bridge the gap between alchemy and modern science</note> called it the *Alkaest* liquor, and described it thus: The Alkaest liquor dissolves every visible and tangible body into its first matter, while preserving the power of the seeds; concerning which the Chemists say: "the common people burn by fire, we burn by water."

*It pleases us to call it "mercurial oil of salt, putrefied and distilled by alembic" <note>an alchemical still</note>: by alchemical license, like those who can give names to their own offspring as they wish. For it is an oil exalted to a higher degree of fiery quality, just as the foundation of every metallic solution (which should be well noted) is oil, without which nothing useful can be achieved in the art. It plays the role of the female in our work, and is rightly called the "bride of the sun" and the "womb" <note>original: *matrix*</note>; it is the hidden key itself for unlocking the closed gates of the metals. Indeed, it dissolves calcined <note>burned to ash</note> metals, it calcines them, it putrefies them, it makes them volatile and spiritual, it tinges them into all colors, and it is the beginning, middle, and end of the tinctures <note>the agents of transmutation</note>. It is of one nature with Gold, as VILLANOVA <note>Arnald of Villanova, a 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> asserts, except that the nature of Gold is complete, digested, and fixed; but the nature of the Water is incomplete, undigested, and volatile. In a word: it is the Fire of the Philosophers, with which the Tree of Hermes is burned to ashes. Concerning this Fire, JOHN PONTANUS says in his Epistle: The Fire of the Philosophers is not the fire of a bath <note>a water bath</note> nor of manure <note>decaying dung used for steady, gentle heat</note>, nor of any other kind which the Philosophers have placed in their books. It is mineral, it is equal, it is continuous, it does not evaporate unless it is over-excited; it partakes of sulfur, it is taken from somewhere other than the matter itself, it separates all things, dissolves, calcines, and congeals, and it—*

<summary>This page lists the many cryptic names given to the alchemical solvent (the "mercurial water") by various masters, eventually identifying it as the "Fire of the Philosophers" that acts as the universal key to metallic transformation.</summary>

This is the fire with a moderate heat; it is a shortcut without any expense, or at least a very small one, because with it the entire work is completed. Therefore, study there, for if I had found this first, I would not have erred two hundred times before I arrived at the practice. For this reason they err, have erred, and will err, because the Philosophers did not place the proper agent in their books—except for one, who is named ARTEPHIUS <note>A legendary alchemist, often claimed to have lived for a thousand years by using the elixir of life.</note>, but he speaks for himself; and unless I had read ARTEPHIUS and understood his meaning, I would never have reached the completion of the work, etc. *Consult him yourself as well, and you will know what our solvent liquid <note>original: menstruum</note> is: I have said enough.*

# CHAPTER VII.
## *Whether the solvent liquid is corrosive?*

GEBER <note>The Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century Persian polymath and the father of early chemistry.</note> seems to be of this opinion in *The Sum of Perfection*, chapter 52, when he says: "Everything that is dissolved must necessarily have the nature of salt, alum, and things similar to them." *And PARACELSUS <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist known for revolutionizing medicine.</note> in Book 4 of the Archidoxes, concerning the Fifth Essence, says thus at the very beginning:* "It is difficult, and hardly believable, to extract the Fifth Essence from metals without a corrosive, especially indeed from Gold, which cannot be overcome except through a corrosive, by which the Fifth Essence and the body are separated from each other, which corrosive can again be removed from it." *And in chapter 3 of On the Long Life, volume 6, book 3, he speaks thus:* "Dissolve gold together with the entire substance of gold with a corrosive, etc., and do this until it becomes one with the corrosive; do not be stunned by this labor, because for gold, if it is gold, a corrosive is beneficial, and without a corrosive it is dead."

It must be known, however, *that our solvent <note>original: menstruum</note> poured over gold ought not properly be called corrosive, but rather fiery, the power and virtue of whose Secret <note>original: Arcani</note> overcomes all poisons; since all Realgar <note>In this context, the author defines Realgar as "living Mercury" (quicksilver) rather than the mineral arsenic sulfide.</note> (that is, living and sublimated Mercury, and also precipitated Mercury) must die in the Elixir of the Sun <note>The "Sun" refers to gold.</note>, and they become a singular and supreme tincture. While also through our solvent a violent dissolution does not occur—such as happens through strong waters <note>original: aquas fortes; nitric acid</note>, royal waters <note>original: regias; aqua regia, a mix of nitric and hydrochloric acid</note>, resuscitating waters, and others of this form—but as was said above in chapter 4, it happens mildly and sweetly without any noise, and with the preservation of its radical moisture, with whose spirits (as LULLY <note>Raymond Lull, a 13th-century philosopher to whom many alchemical texts were later attributed.</note> has it in his Vade mecum) the life-giving power is infused into the materials.*

# CHAPTER VIII.
## *On the Practice of the Stone.*

*After you have obtained the staining soul of the Planet <note>The "soul" or essence of the metal being worked upon.</note>, or its true Fifth Essence, by means of a previous putrefaction <note>The stage of "blackness" or decay required to release the essence of matter.</note>, in which the true Mercury and the true Sulfur of the Philosophers lie hidden, then your material is prepared, so that from it may be made the Stone—*

<meta>catchword: Stone—</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the nature of the alchemical solvent (menstruum), debating whether it is truly "corrosive" in the sense of harsh acids or a "fiery" agent that dissolves gold gently. It cites authorities like Geber, Paracelsus, and Artephius to explain that this secret agent is the key to extracting the Fifth Essence and creating the Philosophers' Stone.</summary>

<keywords>Artephius, Geber, Paracelsus, Lully, menstruum, solvent, corrosive, Fifth Essence, gold, Realgar, elixir, alchemical practice</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the practical instructions for the final stages of the Great Work. The text transition suggests the completion of the preparation of the "Blessed Stone" and the beginning of its "Augmentation" or multiplication in power. The catchword from the previous page "subli-" likely referred to "sublimation," while the current page begins mid-sentence, completing the word "Stone" (Lapidem).</meta>

...our blessed Stone. Take therefore in the name of him *who spoke, and they were made* <note>original: "qui dixit, & facta sunt"; a Biblical reference to God's creation in Genesis</note>, a sufficient quantity of this purest matter. Place it in a fixatory vessel, or a vial, or the Philosophical Egg <term>ovum Philosophicum; a hermetically sealed glass vessel used for long-term heating</term>, and set it in the athanor <term>athanor; a specialized alchemical furnace designed to provide a constant, uniform heat</term>, as you know. It must be kept in a suitable and continuous digestion (for if the heat fails, it is necessary that death follows, or an "abortion" occurs) <note>In alchemy, an "abortion" refers to a failed transformation where the "embryo" of the Stone is ruined by temperature fluctuations.</note>. This heat should be gentle, subtle, altering, and not burning (to speak as the Count <note>Likely referring to Bernard Trevisan, the Count of the March, a 15th-century alchemist.</note> does) from the first conjunction until the perfect washing. Proceed by the governance of the fire—concerning which the ANONYMOUS PHILALETHA <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemical author</note> may be further consulted, who clearly describes the various colors through the governance of each Planet—by coagulating and fixing the matter into the White or Red Stone. For as RAYMUND LULLY <note>Ramon Llull, a major figure in medieval alchemy and logic.</note> warns: he who lacks power and patience in the work will ruin the process through haste.

The sign of the completed work will be this: if the Stone, when cast upon a glowing plate of Venus <note>original: "Veneris laminam"; a plate of copper.</note>, flows like wax, does not smoke, but penetrates and tinges <note>original: "tingat"; the alchemical term for transforming a base metal into gold or silver by "dyeing" its inner nature.</note>. And then the Oriental King is born, sitting in his kingdom and powerful over all the Princes of the world. Hence the Philosopher exclaims: *Come forth from hell, rise again from the tomb, awake from the darkness, for you are clothed in spirituality and clarity, since the voice of resurrection has been heard, and the soul of life has entered into you.* <note>This poetic passage equates the chemical stabilization of the Stone with a spiritual resurrection.</note> Let the Most High be praised, whose gifts yield to the glory of His Most Holy Name and the benefit of our neighbor.

## CHAPTER IX.
### *On the Augmentation of the Blessed Stone.*

Now that you have become a possessor of the aforementioned incombustible, purple-red Sulfur by the help of Divinity, it remains for you to know how to augment it through the repeated "turning of the wheel" (*as the Philosophers say*), beneath which no small mystery of the art is contained. We can augment this by the same method by which we fashioned it. For it must be known that the more often our Sulfur—which is our Stone—is sprinkled or fed with its own milk, dissolved in a dewy bath <note>original: "balneo rorido"; likely a "Balneum Mariae" or water bath providing moist heat.</note>, and again coagulated and fixed as in the first work, the greater its power of tinging shall be. Thus, where after the completion of the first work one part tinged one hundred parts of purified Mercury or another imperfect metal, in the second solution through the "virgin’s milk" <note>original: "lac virginis"; a symbolic name for a specific alchemical solvent, often a mercurial distillate.</note> and its coagulation and fixation, one part will tinge a thousand. And so, through repeated turns, our medicine is augmented and multiplied in quantity and quality, in power and weight.

Therefore, take one part of our Stone and pour on two parts of "virgin's milk," or the oil of mercurial salt—putrefied and distilled through an alembic. Dissolve and coagulate it as was done in the first work; and our water, which before was only a mineral in potential, becomes in actuality a metal more precious than gold. Thus is the Stone made wondrous through...

...sublimations, and it is revived through imbibitions <term>imbibitiones; the process of adding moisture to dry matter to facilitate its transformation</term>, which is the supreme Universal path. Once these steps have been led to the desired end, projection <term>proiectio; the final stage where a small amount of the Stone is cast upon molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver</term> can be carried out at your pleasure upon this or that prepared and decently purified molten metal, according to whether you have obtained the white tincture <note>for silver</note> or the red tincture <note>for gold</note>. The true use of this process will be taught in abundance by the art and all the books of the Philosophers (especially our PHILALETHA <note>Referring to Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist whose pseudonym means "peaceful lover of truth."</note>).

# CHAPTER X.

->On the medical use of the Stone, both internal and external.<-

It should be known regarding this blessed Stone <note>original: "Lapis benedictus"</note> that it is a Universal medicine <note>original: "medicina Catholica"</note>, containing within itself the perfect cure for all diseases, whether hot or cold, insofar as they are recognized as curable by nature and permitted by GOD to be healed. If you ask how this most perfect medicine, this celestial tincture, and other similar panaceas <term>panacea; a legendary remedy capable of curing all ills</term> act and operate by curing opposites within the human body? We respond that all of this is achieved by warming, illuminating, and radiating the Archaeus <term>Archaeus; in Paracelsian medicine, the vital force or "internal chemist" of the body that governs health and digestion</term>, as our philosopher von HELMONT <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneer of chemical medicine and a follower of Paracelsus.</note> says in the book and treatise titled: *Great virtue exists in Herbs, Words, and Stones.* <note>original: "In Herbis, Verbis, & Lapidibus est magna virtus."</note> Indeed, they act without their own dissolution or destruction, without penetration, admission, mixing, or change; they act freely, even from a distance, upon the unawakened or kindled Archaeus. They act merely, as it were, by their sight, irradiation, or the emission of their powers produced within the medium, while their original weight and properties remain retained and unchanged. Thus, as JOACHIM POLEMANN <note>A 17th-century chemist and prominent commentator on Van Helmont’s theories.</note> so clearly states, they transmute the spirits of darkness—namely diseases (which are nothing other than the properties of the bed of death or precursors of dark death)—into good spirits, such as they were before when the person was healthy. *And through this renewal of weakened powers, strength is simultaneously restored to the whole once again.*

The dose is 1 to 2 grains, according to the age and strength of the sick person, in a small draft of warm wine, or dissolved in one spoonful of its quintessence <term>quinta essentia; the "fifth essence" or purest, most concentrated medicinal extract</term>, administered every third day. In external diseases, wounds, malignant ulcers <note>original: "ulceribus cochöericis," likely a variation of cacoethicis, meaning stubborn or malignant</note>, spreading sores <note>original: "phagadænicis"</note>, fistulas, gangrene, cancer, etc., one grain is administered in wine daily or every other day. The externally affected part is washed with wine in which a portion of our Stone has been dissolved, or if necessity demands, it is injected through a syringe, with a lead plate and a suitable bandage placed over it.

And here is the completed internal use of this great mystery; for its attainment, invoke the Light of Light <note>A reference to the Nicene Creed, used here to suggest the spiritual source of alchemical insight</note>, and seek the illumination of your intellect with a pure heart, and you shall receive it. Then work sufficiently, bring aid to the poor, do not abuse the gifts of GOD, believe the Gospel, and be pious. Amen.

H." for Sigillo Hermetis; a method of airtight sealing by melting the glass neck of a flask</note>. For he who does not have power and patience in the work, as RAYMOND LULLY <note>Raimundus Lullius; a major medieval philosopher and purported alchemist</note> warns, ruins the work through haste.

This will be the sign of the completed work: if the Stone, when cast upon a glowing plate of Venus <note>original: "Veneris laminam"; Venus is the alchemical symbol for copper</note>, flows like wax, does not smoke, but penetrates and tinges <term>tingat; the act of chemically transforming and coloring a base metal into gold or silver</term>. And then the Eastern King is born, sitting in his kingdom and powerful over all the Princes of the world. Hence the Philosopher exclaims:
> Go forth from hell, rise again from the tomb, awaken from the darkness, for you are clothed in spirituality and clarity, because the voice of resurrection has been heard, and the soul of life has entered into you. Praise be to the Most High, whose gifts yield to the glory of His most Holy Name and the benefit of one's neighbor.

# CHAPTER IX.
## On the Augmentation of the Blessed Stone.

When you have now become the possessor of the aforementioned incombustible, purple-red Sulfur by the help of the Divinity, it remains for you to know how to increase it through the repeated "inversion of the wheel" (as the Philosophers say), beneath which a significant mystery of the art revolves. Moreover, we can increase the Stone by the same method by which we composed it. It must be known that the more often our Sulfur, which is our Stone, is moistened or fed with its own milk, dissolved in a dewy bath <term>balneo rorido; a vapor bath or gentle heat source</term>, and coagulated and fixed again as in the first work, the greater its future power of tinging shall always be. Indeed, whereas after the first work is finished, one part tinged one hundred parts of purified Mercury or another imperfect metal, in the second dissolution through the "virgin milk" and its coagulation and fixation, one part will tinge a thousand. And thus by repeated turns, our medicine is augmented and multiplied in quantity and quality, in virtue and weight.

Take therefore one part of our Stone, and pour on two parts of "virgin milk" <note>An alchemical solvent, often a mercurial water</note>, or the oil of putrefied and distilled Mercurial salt. Dissolve and coagulate it, as was done in the first work, and our water, which before was only mineral in potential, becomes in act a metal more precious than gold; and thus the Stone is made marvelous <note>original: "merificatur," likely a scribal error for "mirificatur" or "purificatur"</note> through subli-

# CHAPTER X.
## *On the Internal and External Medical Use of the Stone.*

It must be known concerning this blessed Stone that it is a Catholic medicine <note>original: "medicina Catholica"; here used in the sense of a "universal" remedy for all ailments</note>, containing within itself the perfect cure for all diseases, whether hot or cold, provided they are recognized by nature as curable and are permitted by GOD to be healed. If you ask how this most perfect medicine, this celestial tincture, and other similar panaceas <term>panacea; a legendary remedy capable of curing all physical ills</term> act and work by curing contraries in the human body? We respond that all this is achieved by warming, illuminating, and irradiating the Archeus <term>Archeus; in Paracelsian medicine, the vital "internal chemist" or life force that governs the body’s health</term>, as our Philosopher von HELMONT <note>Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644), a foundational figure in chemistry and medicine</note> has it in the book and treatise titled: *In Herbs, Words, and Stones is great power.* <note>original: "in Herbis, Verbis, & Lapidibus est magna virtus"</note> For they act without being dissolved or destroyed, without penetration, admission, mixture, or change, acting freely—even from a distance—upon the unawakened or kindled Archeus. They work as if by their mere presence, irradiation, or the emission of their powers produced in the medium, while their original weight and properties remain unchanged and retained. In this way—as the excellent JOACHIM POLEMANNUS <note>A 17th-century writer known for explaining Helmont’s theories on the spiritual nature of medicine</note> states—they transmute the spirits of darkness, namely diseases (which are all nothing else than the properties of the bed of death or precursors of dark death), into good spirits, such as they were before when the man was healthy. And by this renewal of failing powers, strength is simultaneously restored to the whole body once again.

The dose is 1 to 2 grains <term>grain; a traditional unit of weight based on a single seed of cereal, roughly 65 milligrams</term> according to the age and strength of the sick person, in a small draught of warm wine, or dissolved in one spoonful of its quintessence <term>quintessence; the most concentrated and purified form of a substance</term>, and administered every third day. In external diseases, wounds, malignant ulcers, spreading sores <note>original: "phagadænicis"; refers to ulcers that "eat" or destroy the surrounding flesh</note>, fistulas, gangrene, cancer, etc., one grain shall be administered in wine daily or every other day. The externally affected part, however, is washed with wine in which a portion of our Stone has been dissolved, or if necessity requires, it is injected through a syringe <note>original: "syphonem"</note>, with a lead plate placed over it and a suitable bandage.

And this is the internal use of this great and perfected mystery; for the attainment of which, invoke the Light of Light, and seek the illumination of your intellect with a pure heart, and you shall receive it. Then work sufficiently, bring aid to the poor, do not abuse the gifts of GOD, believe the Gospel, and be pious. Amen.

<header>CONCERNING THE REGERMINATION OF SILVER discovered by a new method.</header>

Silver in mines also appears to present itself in the form of hairs, as witnessed by Oswald Croll <note>original: "Oswald. Croll."; a famous alchemist and professor of medicine (1563–1609)</note> in *The Tree of Man*, Book 1, Chapter 7, Section 5, Paragraph 18; and also in the form of a natural little tree, according to Francesco Imperato <note>Napoleonic era naturalist</note> in *Natural Discourses*, Book 2, page 16, and Dr. Johannes Gerhard in *Universal Medicine: Assertions and Defense*, page 23. Indeed, it even takes the form of a Vine, such as we believe Lord Olaus Worm <note>Ole Worm (1588–1654), a Danish physician and antiquarian</note> possessed; you may consult his *Museum*, Book 7, Section 3, Chapter 2, folio 116. What more? A natural Silver Cross was seen and handled by our own hands—that very cross which, like a vegetable, grew out of the ore, and is displayed in the Cabinet of Curiosities <term>Technophylacium; a princely collection of art and natural wonders</term> of our most Illustrious Prince and Elector in Dresden.

Just as this natural germination is nothing other than vegetable growth from its own power, mediated by heat and moisture, so the Hermeticists <note>Alchemists following the tradition of Hermes Trismegistus</note> were always anxious to find a way in which, imitating nature, they themselves could make metals germinate outside their mines. Indeed, Lord Cassius Fasket, a Silesian Physician and Alchemist to the Emperor, once attempted this, and he is said to have progressed so far as to display a Philosophical Tree of Metals. He communicated the "process" (as they call it) or the method for making it to the most August Emperor of that time. MANUSCRIPTS in my possession—which I have compared in Latin more than once—leave no room for doubt that this process was taken from the short treatise by Petrus Ribola, a Monk of the Benedictine Order, *On the Germinating Power of Metals*, which appears with Burggravius’s *Biolychnium* (Frankfurt, 1630, in octavo).

Indeed, I cannot hide that I observe some difficulty regarding that process, since I see that for such germination there is required: (I.) that which germinates—namely any Metal you like, except Mercury; (II.) a Philosophical Earth in which it ought to germinate, which is the Stellated Regulus of Antimony <term>Stellatus Antimonii Regulus; a purified form of antimony that forms star-like crystalline patterns upon cooling</term>; (III.) the heat of fire as the efficient cause, which is both actual and potential; (IV.) and finally, moisture, namely Mercury, through which the "imbibition" <note>the soaking or absorption of a liquid into a solid</note> occurs. Nor can I deny that I have produced that Tree more than once, but in the common way. For when "Luna" <note>original: "Luna"; the alchemical name for Silver</note> (half an ounce) was dissolved in spirit of Nitre <note>Nitric acid</note>, and I poured in spring water (4 ounces), I threw in common Mercury (1 or 2 ounces). Finally, once mixed, I took great care not to disturb it, and I saw the Metal send up pleasant little branches and shrubs that filled the cavity of the glass.

Likewise, Johann Rudolf Glauber <note>1604–1670, a famous German-Dutch alchemist and chemist</note> has followed this at length in Part 2 of *Philosophical Furnaces*, Chapter 85, and also in *On the Salt of Art*, page 63, stating that the growth of metals happens through the "liquor of flints" <term>Liquor Silicum; potassium silicate, often used in "chemical garden" experiments</term>. Regarding this matter, to say what I think, the same Liquor can, and with easier...

-># OBSERVATION<-

...can be obtained with much less labor and at lower cost if you melt one-third of a pound of purified potash <note>original: "Ciner. Clavell. purg."; potassium carbonate derived from wood ashes</note> and one-quarter of a pound of calcined flint <note>original: "Silic. calcin."</note> in a crucible into a single mass, and then allow it to liquefy in a cellar <note>A process of deliquescence where the solid salt absorbs moisture from the air to become a liquid</note>.

Furthermore, since those "Germinations" I mentioned do not consist of metals alone, but also of Antimony, Mercury, or even "Liquor of Flints" <note>original: "Liquore Silicum"; a solution of potassium silicate</note> mixed together, I began to turn my every thought toward inquiring into some "Medium" <note>an intervening substance or agent</note> by which it might happen that, just as in the bowels of the earth by the work of Nature, so by the work of the craftsman, shrubs might truly re-germinate into pure metal. Since I consider it well enough established that in the three kingdoms of nature <note>Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral</note> a "tempered acid" <note>original: "Acidum temperatum"; a balanced acidic spirit</note> especially aids the vegetation of all things, and I had detected this same quality in common Sulfur itself, I finally turned my attention to that mineral. It was so far from failing the hope I had conceived of it that it instead provided me with the opportunity for the following experiment in Regermination. I reduced plates of Luna (that is, refined silver) or pure silver coins into a powder by calcining them with common Sulfur. I then placed these in a crucible over glowing coals and added several small pieces of sulfur from time to time. Once this was burnt off, I did not stop doing the same until the silver I had calcined had absorbed as much of that acid from the sulfur as was sufficient. Now, when the silver mass contained in the crucible had endured the fire of the wheel <term>Rotæ ignem; a surrounding, circular heat used in alchemical furnaces</term> for nearly an hour, behold! Several very small silver shrubs and branches, which were quite hard, gradually began to rise to a height that barely or not at all exceeded the width of a thumb. Thus, I achieved the first signs of Regermination quite successfully.

It is the nature of the human mind that, not content with new discoveries, it strives to improve what it has once brought forth; being conscious of secrets, it always loves to progress further. You will not be surprised, **READER**, that the same happened to me here. For while I was uniquely fixed on this care and applied myself more to the matter, the initial letters of my name, **C. A. B.** <note>Christian Adolph Balduin (1632–1682), a noted alchemist and official</note>, appeared to me. From these, I observed with wonder that pure silver began to sprout and stretch upward in the form of both little trees and grass. These were identical in every way to those very leaves and "hairs" which nature herself produced under the earth; as is not unknown to those skilled in metallic matters, these are of the rarest occurrence. However, this whole matter of Regermination, and how it ought to be properly begun or by what fire it should be ruled, is something that one wishes to be shown through sight rather than taught through words.

I **NOTE** from this: (I.) that our common Sulfur consists not only of the "fatness of the earth," <note>original: "pinguedine telluris"; an alchemical term for the oily, combustible principle of a substance</note> and resin and oil (which both ancient and modern writers...

He records a personal success where the silver sprouted into shapes resembling his own initials.</summary>

### CONCERNING THE REGERMINATION OF SILVER
### discovered by a new artifice.

I see that philosophers maintain—indeed, [this sulfur] is rightly deemed the invisible fire of Nature (an assertion to which Kircher <note>Athanasius Kircher (1602–1680), a Jesuit polymath who wrote extensively on geology and alchemy</note>, a most profound author in other respects, very wisely and willingly subscribes). But I also see that they hold it to possess a certain vitriolic acidity <note>original: "aciditate Chalcantosâ"; referring to chalcantum, an old term for vitriol or copperas</note>; and this is not without reason: since, besides Gold, it calcines all metals and produces Vitriol. For what is any Vitriol other than Sulfur that has been deflagrated <note>burned away or consumed by fire</note>? And once the Sulfur has deflagrated, it can be increased in a visible way. Moreover, it is necessary that this affinity—which exists between the Acid of Sulfur (the "First Being" <note>original: "primô Ente"; the essential principle or quintessence</note> of Vitriol) and Silver—be of the highest degree, since it works so wonderfully for its regermination and growth in the manner we have already described. As long as these things remain so, that statement in the *Correctorium* of Richard the Englishman <note>A 14th-century alchemical text often attributed to Richardus Anglicus</note> seems far from the truth: *“Every common sulfur is contrary to metals,”* and so forth. Likewise, his other claim: *“How can it inspire life into other metals? For it has a double superfluity: an inflammable substance and an earthy dross.”*

(II.) We consider it to be placed beyond all controversy that the true cause of the Vegetation of metals is Acid, whether they are dug out from their own bowels or lie hidden beneath the earth. For if, to give an example, you add too little of that Acid to Silver, it will plainly not grow. If, however, you give it enough, but pay no heed to the method of Fire and cause the heat to be too intense, then the Acid that was present will fly away more quickly; the Regermination will keep you in suspense and utterly abandon all hope. Now, as to the way in which Sulfur can be both called and truly be the principle of natural things—especially of metals and minerals—and by what means it concurs in the generation of all sublunary things; in how many ways; in what places Sulfur is made and generated; how many species there are; and what their differences are: the most learned Kircher has explained all these things excellently and with care in *The Subterranean World* <note>original: "Mundo Subterraneo"; Athanasius Kircher’s 1665 encyclopedia of the geocosm</note>, *Book 9, Section 3, page 146, and Book 11, Section 2, page 252.*

(III.) It follows now that Silver melted in a just and competent fire does not lose its life, since it can be roused anew through the Acid of Sulfur. Indeed, let us grant this: that Gold and Silver, once removed from their mine, no longer live in the "second act" <note>original: "secundô actu"; a philosophical term referring to the active exercise of a function (like a plant growing), as opposed to the mere potential for it</note>; just as a seed plucked from its herb, or grain stored in a granary, no longer greens, flourishes, or lives. Yet, who is so unskilled in natural things as to deny that life is present within it in the "first act" <note>original: "actu primô"; the latent power or potentiality for life</note>, excited by a vegetative spirit? Supporting us is Fortunius Licetus <note>A 17th-century physician and philosopher</note>, in his work *On the Spontaneous Origin of Living Things, Book 4, Chapter 72*, citing Aristotle’s book *On Marvelous Things Heard*. He reports that Gold, when buried or cast into the earth, has sometimes grown larger. Not only does the experiment we have shown provide credit to this matter, but so too does that Philosophical Tree <note>original: "Philosof. illa Arbor"; also known as Diana’s Tree, a branching growth of silver crystals</note>, recently mentioned, in

O 2
<meta>which...</meta>

### OBSERVATION CONCERNING REGERMINATION

...in producing which the Spirit of the Earth (that is, Saltpeter <note>original: "Nitrum"</note>), Silver likewise, and Mercury work together, and within the interval of a few hours, they produce most delightful plants.

Indeed (IV.), since Sulfur is clearly a singular and admirable product of Nature, Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note> was not ignorant of its powers, writing shortly after the beginning of his *Book of Minerals*, chapter 7:

> It should be known that (unless God prevents it) a wonderful work is contained within Sulfur, and so excellent that the natural light of man is stupefied. Likewise: such power and faculty reside in Sulfur that no expert can exhaust it; such is its virtue that it is a source of wonder to everyone. (Book I and V of *The Nature of Things* <note>original: "de Nat. Rerū"</note>).

Nor indeed was it only in that sulfurous globe of mine—which I described not long ago in the *Treatise on the Gold of the Breeze* <note>original: "Tract. de Auro Auræ"</note>, page 42—that the impulsive, preservative, directing, turning, sounding, heating, and finally shining power was shown to me. But even in these very days, I observed that although sulfur is otherwise combustible by our fire, it is also set on fire and liquefied by the rays of the Sun <note>the author is likely referring to literal sunlight here, though "Sol" often means Gold in alchemy</note>, collected through a parabolic mirror <note>term: a curved mirror used to concentrate sunlight into a single point of intense heat</term>; yet it is not consumed, and nothing at all of its weight is lost. Since the reason for this is not obvious to just anyone, I would hardly expect an explanation from anyone. Concerning its virtues in medicine, I shall not speak here, for there exists a treatise by Unzer on Sulfur, and not a few authors are cited there to whom we may direct those interested in the matter. 

Nevertheless, it must finally be investigated: whether from that common Sulfur, that Acid—which is otherwise the "First Being" <note>term: "Ens" – the essential substance or quintessence</note> of Vitriol—could be extracted more copiously and purely in some other way, and the generation of metals be procured from it? And,

(V.) if not from the specific Acid of Sulfur, then perhaps at least from the most Universal [Acid] <note>likely referring to the "Universal Spirit" or "Azoch" sought by alchemists to animate matter</note>, an increase might be added to metals, and a certain "domestic" and secret—so to speak—Metal Mine <note>original: "Metallifodina"</note> might be established. For if (as we have already mentioned above) you keep that [common] Philosophical Tree <note>term: "Arborem Philosophicam" – a crystalline structure of silver and mercury that grows in a flask, resembling a tree; also known as Diana's Tree</note> for a full year, and indeed keep it undisturbed, you will see that the Silver has grown. The cause of this increase is the Mercury, which has lost as much of its own nature as has been added to it, having been changed into silver. 

Regarding this matter, see Master Johann Hiskias Cardalucius, in his commentary on Lazarus Ercker's *Subterranean Hall* <note>original: "Aula subterranea"; a seminal work on metallurgy and mining</note>, Book 1, page 89. Indeed, it is known that from a certain fertile ore of Gold <note>original: "Solis"; the Sun is the alchemical symbol for Gold</note> and some liquor, with monthly additions of the same, a perpetual increase of Gold grew for a certain Noblewoman:

> The Gold first rising like grass, of a green color; then gradually the tips turned into Gold <note>original: "Solem"</note>, which could be harvested with scissors. 

As reported by Nicolaus Niger Happelius in *The Heliconian Hand-guide to Philosophical Gold* <note>original: "Cheiragog. Hel. de Aur. Philos."</note>, page 38.

# ON SILVER DISCOVERED BY A NEW ARTIFICE
o 3
<meta>catchword: M. Zuer.</meta>

But let these things suffice for now; for neither do my duties—with which I am frequently overwhelmed in public affairs—nor my private, and indeed more taxing, chemical labors allow for more. There will be men from our illustrious College of the Curious in Nature <note>The *Academia Naturae Curiosorum*, now known as the Leopoldina, the oldest continuously existing learned society in the world</note> who are most skilled in the art of judging, who will know these matters better than I, and will contemplate them with every sharpness of intellect. Nature is not yet fully occupied: many things still lie hidden in the well of Democritus <note>A classical proverb: Democritus said truth lies at the bottom of a well, meaning it is difficult to find</note>, to be dragged into the light more by the sweat of those working than by the genius of speculators, however profound. It will be yours, **READER**, to follow whatever is most right, and not to prefer the unproven to the proven.

<margin>*Lazare de Baïf on vessels; Homer's Iliad, Book 4; Herodian, Book 2; Athanasius Kircher in "Latium," p. 68 from Marliani; Fortunato Liceti, Book 2, ch. 55; Jacob Guther on the Law of the Manes <note>The spirits of the dead</note>, Book 1, ch. 26; Philippe Hérault, Book 1, ch. 7; Isaac Casaubon's notes on Suetonius's "Augustus"; Johann Kirchmann on Roman Funerals, Book 3, ch. 8.*</margin>
Was it a very careful diligence, or perhaps a superstition, that the Ancient Romans displayed in arranging the ashes of their own people (whom they cremated after death)? Urns were employed for this purpose. Nor are monuments of this most ancient rite more frequent anywhere on earth than in Italy.
