# CHAPTER VII.

Our **Solvent** <term>Menstruum: From the Latin *menstruus* (monthly), this refers to a specialized alchemical solvent used to break down substances into their original components.</term> can be called, primarily by this name, the **Most Universal Magnet**, because by attracting both the **Sun** <note>original: ☉ (the astrological and alchemical symbol for Gold or the Sun)</note> and all other metals, it dissolves them fundamentally and without any force; moreover, it uniquely loves conjunction with the perfect metals.

# CHAPTER VII.
->Concerning the Time for Attracting the Gold of the Air.<-

The author **Marsilio Ficino, Canon of Florence** <note>Ficino (1433–1499) was a central figure of the Italian Renaissance who translated Plato and sought to reconcile Hermetic philosophy with Christianity.</note>, in his books *On the Triple Life*, (who kept an image of Plato alone in his bedchamber and perpetually hung a burning lamp before it day and night, as recorded by *Harsting in Dryad. Eccles. ch. 12, p. 75*) is particularly insistent on this point: *that in reconciling celestial Graces, whether for the matter of metals or herbs, etc., the Moon must be considered above all else, so that it may be in its own mansion, suitable for the desired work, and in a sign and aspect favorable to that grace which you strive for and seek. Next, the Sun should be joined to the Moon in a favorable aspect, being in the house of that Planet and in a favorable aspect to that grace whose influence you are procuring. And so forth.*

What of the fact that **P. Schottus** <note>Caspar Schott (1608–1666), a Jesuit physicist and mathematician famous for his works on "curious" technology and mechanics.</note> (in *Technica Curiosa, book 9, proposition 26*), when about to apply a pointer to chronometric instruments, explains that in **Spagyric** <term>Spagyric: The alchemical process of separating and then recombining the components of a substance to purify it.</term> work, the times for choosing the Hermetic matter are strictly determined?

Nor should it be silenced in this place that not so long ago, a certain **Enigma** was secretly communicated to me, in which this sentiment is found: *Seek: What kind of Animal? In the Northern region of the sky is seen an Animal marked among others by seven bright stars, which has a correspondence with a terrestrial animal of the same nature. Let it be taken while Mars is in the house of the Sun, whose daughter is fed by the blood of her brothers: each one being in its own domicile so that it may grow: and this while the Sun is in the house of Mars. This young girl is submerged by her own dew, dwelling in her own mansion by the number of the maternal celestial signature.* <note>This "Animal" likely refers to the constellation Ursa Major (the Great Bear) or the North Star, which alchemists often associated with the "Steel" or the magnetic center of their work.</note> But we consider it a matter of religious duty to expose or profane this stumbling block to the worthy and unworthy alike; for whoever the Author here may be, he wished this matter—certainly a secret of no common importance—to be primarily concealed.

I cannot help but mention, however, along with **Galileo Galilei, Florentine Nobleman**, in his *New-Ancient Works of the Most Holy Fathers, p. 56*: *I do not believe that it would be far from sound philosophizing to say that he [the Sun], as the supreme Minister of Nature, and in a certain way the soul and heart of the world, infuses into the other* <note>original Italian: "Non credo, che sara lontano dal ben' filosofare, il dire, Che egli, come Ministro massimo della Natura, & in certo modo, Anima è cuore del Mondo, infonde agl' altri..."</note>

<header>CONCERNING THE TIME FOR ATTRACTING THE GOLD OF THE AIR. 141</header>

...bodies that surround it, not only Light, but also Motion, etc.

<margin>Alexander de Vicentinis, *On the Influence of the Heavens upon Sublunary Things*, ch. V.</margin>
For he has proved through many arguments that the heavens exert their influence through light and motion alone.

<margin>Dionysius the Areopagite in the book *On the Divine Names*.</margin>
He described at greater length the marvelous power and energy of the sun.

Therefore, the Sphere of the Sun awakens life-giving heat in two ways: first and principally by the Sun, the greatest of the Stars, illuminating all things with its rays; and secondly, through its perennial and continuous motion, which agitates the enclosed bodies.

<margin>Pierre Borel, *Medical-Physical Observations*, 3.</margin>
1. However much the higher air or breeze <note>original: *aura vel aër superior*</note> is ignited daily by the Sun—from which the Light that makes the day takes its origin—it is much more subtle than that ignited from "water of life" <note>original: *aqua vitæ*, likely referring to distilled alcohol or a potent chemical distillate</note>. Yet, because the heat of the season does not flourish in the same way at all times (for in Autumn and Winter it is almost sluggish and extinguished, while in Spring and Summer it grows and intensifies), it has been believed even until today that the cause of this warmth is not so much the celestial sign itself, but the proximity and directness of the Sun above our heads. Thus, the **Solar Sulfur**, or the **Gold of the Air** <term>Gold of the Air: Also known as *Aurum Aurae*, it refers to a vital, celestial spirit or "nitre" believed to be present in the atmosphere, which alchemists sought to condense into a physical substance.</term>, is attracted most abundantly and directly when the Sun enters the sign of **Cancer**, or **Virgo**, or best of all, **Leo** <note>In astrology, Leo is the "House of the Sun," where its influence is considered most potent.</note>, at any time, whether in the morning or even at midday. I can here once again prove this by my own example, if the matter so requires. For on the 10th day of March in the year 1673, when the Sun had entered **Aries**, I set out my usual **Magnet** <note>The "Magnet" refers to a prepared chemical salt or substance designed to absorb the "vital spirit" from the air.</note>, but I found scarcely a palm's breadth—as they say—or even a trace of the **Green Lion** <term>Green Lion: A metaphorical alchemical term for the raw, acidic energy of nature or a specific mineral solvent (often vitriol or antimony) used to "consume" and dissolve metals.</term>, and it hardly changed at all from its color, which had been white from the beginning. This was despite it being subjected to fire for nearly two hours. On the 29th day of the same month, I took up the same experiment again and achieved a greater power of the Gold of the Air. From this, who would not notice how much importance both the sunniness of the day and the warmer part of the year bring to this work?

2. Regarding the time for attracting the Gold of the lower Air, it must also be held that the moment when the purest saline body attracts the **Spirit of Saltpeter** <note>original: *Nitri Spiritum*; 17th-century philosophers believed saltpeter contained an "aerial nitre" that was the secret food of life and flame.</note> is a matter of great importance. To ensure nothing prevents their conjunction, the dregs must be separated. You will achieve this most correctly only by undertaking the work of putrefaction and sublimation in the time of Spring—that is, with moderate heat.

<margin>Plato, *Commentary of the Four*, book 3, section "Now it will be."</margin>
For the springtime is suitable for sublimation; at least two months, or even more time, must be granted to this matter.

# CHAPTER VII.

At what time during the cooking of the Liquor or our Magnet should Gold or Silver be added to it? It is pleasing, and considered to be of no small benefit, to do so at the very beginning of the cooking. This is because in these perfected Metals, there are "tinging rays" <note>original: *radii tingentes*, referring to the power of a substance to project its color or quality onto another</note> and both red and white <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, which are also found and contained within our <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>. For one always acts as the Magnet for the other, and the solvent remains with the dissolved thing due to the similarity of their substance. And just as the most ancient of Philosophers—always keeping the principle of universality in mind at the beginning and middle—*determined only at the end a Universal purpose destined for a particular goal:* so we constantly assert that the Philosopher’s Stone can be made from the *Alkahest* <term>Alkahest: A hypothetical universal solvent capable of reducing any body to its primary liquid form</term> alone, so that it may cure diseases; which, at the final stage, is combined and mixed with common Gold *for the purpose of transformation—not so that the Stone may be perfected by the Gold, but so that the Gold may be perfected by our Stone itself, and rendered fruitful and alive, so that it may procreate its own likeness and propagate its offspring unto infinity.* On this matter, one might usefully read the *Treatise* of *Pierre Jean Fabre, Physician to the King of France* (which he completed on May 15, 1653, to please FREDERICK, DUKE of Holstein): although I have doubts whether this writing, the like of which perhaps does not exist elsewhere and is therefore extremely rare, has ever seen the light of day.

### CHAPTER VIII.
->On the Colors of the Gold of the Air.<-

The Philosophers have called their most universal Matter, among other things, the "Green Lion." Misled by this, many chemists have sought the subject based on its external color and looked for actual greenness. Some have seized with greedy eyes and hands upon that green foam that floats on stagnant waters in early spring. They chose to call this little bird the "Water-Bird" <note>original: *Wasser-Vogel*</note> and "Adam’s Earth." From it they prepared a volatile spirit, a volatile <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⊖</note>, and a foul-smelling oil, which they claimed was the Matter of the Philosopher’s Stone.

Now, *Dr. Christoph Bauzmann, Ordinary Physician to the Swedish Government at Bremen,* has shared with me the description of a certain bucket, open at the top and filled with rain. By using two bellows, which are positioned there, at the meridian and hottest time of the day when the Sun has entered Leo <note>The astrological sign of Leo (late July/August), when the Sun's power is at its peak</note>, a certain very pure and fatty green matter—which Bauzmann himself believes to be the Gold of the Air (and he places much importance on it)—is attracted in great quantity from the air. However, as I have not yet made a trial of this, I do not dare to interpose my own judgment here: meanwhile, I am preserving the account of that work, which I mentioned, along with the process; which a very great MAN gave to me as a gift to keep not long ago.

### ON THE COLORS OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR.

If we believe *Heinrich Khunrath* at all, in his *Amphitheater of Eternal Wisdom* <note>original: *Theatrum Æternæ Sapientiæ* (1595), a famous alchemical-mystical text</note>, page 147, he mentions a certain <term>Green Lion</term> <note>The "Green Lion" usually refers to a raw, acidic mineral or the vital force within matter; here it describes a specific stage of the universal solvent</note> which was universal. He speaks of its blood—that is, no common Gold—and says he saw it with his own eyes; he even touched it with his hands, tasted it with his tongue, and finally perceived the Gold of the Wise by its scent. I must indeed affirm the same regarding this Gold of the upper Air, or Green Lion, which I claim as my own right. O blessed Greenness, circling through the universe, whose center is everywhere, but whose periphery is spread through all the abysses of nature!

I believe *Johann Agricola* <note>A 17th-century chemist and physician</note> himself will not deny this, writing in his *Commentary on Poppius*, page 15: -> "This Greenness is most correctly turned into our Gold." <- But it seems to me he was not without reason ignorant of the manner in which it ought to be prepared. Yet these words agree perfectly with our own experiment.

For first it takes on greenness; then a blue color, and finally purple. Here I would have you note: if you apply a slightly stronger fire to the <term>Green Lion</term> in the Magnet, it returns to its own chaos and leaves the nest it would otherwise have occupied—leaving the <term>Magnet</term> <note>The "Magnet" here is the substance used to "attract" the vital spirit from the air</note> entirely empty and just as it was before, which you may wonder at not a little—white. Therefore, quickly and while it is still hot, pound the Magnet imbued with the Green Lion in a mortar. If you allow this to be dissolved in spring water, it will become green, yet in such a way that the purple Lion is soon precipitated into a powder, which is washed away. In the same way, if you pour Spirit of Wine <note>Ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note> over the Magnet reduced to powder and allow it to digest gently, it extracts the blood of this Green Lion—or the golden <term>Sulfur</term> in red form; this must be well preserved for preparing the <term>Potable Gold</term> <note>A medicinal gold tincture believed to grant longevity</note> of the Air, about which we shall now begin to speak in what follows.

2. The Gold of the lower Air—that is, our Saltpeter <note>original: *Nitrum nostrum*</note>—also has its color. And indeed, that which grows in Egypt was white and rosy according to the Ancients and Dioscorides; it is mostly reported as red by *Prospero Alpini* in *Egyptian Medicine*, book 4, page 148. The one we use presents itself as white, with a purple soul within (the Hermetic Gold of the Air, lying hidden; but which can be driven out by the artifice of Vulcan <note>The god of fire, representing alchemical heat</note>). Nor is it anything else but the very golden Blood of the Dragon, changeable into a <term>Salamander</term> <note>A mythical lizard said to live in fire; here, a substance that remains stable and "alive" in extreme heat</note>, living in the fire. Regardless of what *Robert Boyle* <note>A pioneer of modern chemistry who often challenged alchemical theories</note> brings forward in his *Physiological Essays*, page 199, to obscure this. And what need is there for words? Is not Saltpeter (*Chemical Lexicon* by William Johnson, printed in London, 1652), Blood-

# CHAPTER IX.

...that blood-red [color]? Do we not rely on the support of *Dr. Johann Becher*? <note>Johann Joachim Becher (1635–1682), a German chemist known for his theory of "earthy" substances that influenced the phlogiston theory</note> who, in his *Subterranean Physics* <note>original: *Physica subterranea*, Becher's most famous work</note>, book 1, section 5, chapter 2, number 109, ascribes to Sulphur the power of coloring Nitre <note>Saltpeter or potassium nitrate</note>. We need not even call upon the goldsmiths, to whom nothing is more certain than that even crude Nitre contains a Tincture, a Spirit most familiar and pleasing to Gold; for this reason, when it is smeared with water onto pale Gold, it can, in a small fire, bathe it in a friendly tincture and a deep blood-redness.

So also, when our <term>Alkahest</term> <note>A hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any body to its primary liquid matter</note> is cooked by itself, it then acquires perfect colors, among which is whiteness and finally redness: as is most clear through all the Authors who have known our <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> and have perfected its composition, guided by reason and the chemical art.

## CHAPTER IX.
### *On the GOLD OF THE AIR, POTABLE.*

In his *Dedication to the Renewed Bernhard*, Hornius <note>Georg Horn (1620–1670), a historian and author of alchemical works</note> establishes a twofold Medicine of the Philosophers: one is the truly and simply universal Matter (commonly called the Great and Most Universal <term>Catholic Stone</term>), and the other is almost universal in a certain respect—known by another name as the Small Catholic Stone or the Metallic Tincture. He notes that the materials from which these are prepared are different: that the <term>Egyptian Hermes</term> <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> is celebrated as the inventor of the former, and the <term>Arabian Geber</term> <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan, a foundational figure in early chemistry and alchemy</note> as the inventor of the latter. The matter of the former enjoys the celestial Sun; the latter, the terrestrial Sun or Gold. Each uses its own Magnet, by whose help that force is reduced from potentiality (as they say) into action and multiplicative power.

<margin>*Osiander on the Sun, page 119.*</margin>
These things are not to the taste of *Johann Resch* <note>original: *Johanni Reschio*</note> in his *Experiments*, and for that reason he rejects that opinion. But in truth, I certainly do not see how it lacks a foundation in its own respect. For such a close kinship exists between our Mercury and Gold that a Philosopher of great name left it written that *Gold is nothing else than that ethereal Mercury, most highly coagulated in a certain pure place and reduced to perfect fixation*. See the *Preface of Dr. Joel Langelott* <note>A 17th-century physician to the Duke of Holstein-Gottorp</note> in *Dr. Johann Tilemann’s Experiments*. And if you have not yet forgotten what has been revealed so far regarding attraction, best reader, it is believable that these things cannot have slipped from your mind.

1. The Sulphur of the Sun and the Gold of the superior Air spontaneously follow the Magnet. From this, therefore, <term>Potable Gold</term> <note>A medicinal elixir supposedly containing liquid gold, highly sought after as a panacea</note> is prepared, using the precipitated golden Sulphur; wash it, and once joined with the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>The vital force or "anima mundi" believed to permeate the atmosphere</note> according to the rules of the art, let it be fixed. Or, if you are unrefined and ignorant of all these things, distill...

->### ON POTABLE GOLD OF THE AIR.<-

...[distill] the Spirit of wine tinged with the Gold of the Air until only a fourth part remains, and you will have the residue: namely, **Potable Gold**, of a most pleasing appearance due to its redness. Although it initially exhibits a certain saltiness <note>original: *falsedinem*, referring to a salty or brackish taste often found in alchemical residues</note>, which whoever tastes it will easily perceive with the tongue; nevertheless, after several days have passed, it settles like clear water, acting as a <term>Magnet</term> <note>In alchemy, a "magnet" is a substance believed to attract celestial or spiritual virtues from the atmosphere</note>, with the tincture floating on top. Once this is poured off, you should protect it well in glass from the rays of the Sun. For if this Gold is exposed to the Sun, it becomes colorless within a few days. From this, it is clearer than the midday sun that the Sun draws back this Gold of the Air.

And just as in Chapter 3 I asserted that the Soul, distilled from <term>Nitre</term> <note>Saltpeter, often seen as the vehicle for the "universal spirit"</note> with water, is a life-giving force which may be incorporated into a suitable subject: so my "Gold of the Air," as I have called it, can be joined and fixed with that same soul, and from both, a Potable Gold can be made that is more excellent and effective.

2. In like manner, Potable Gold can be made from the "lower Air," or our Nitre: namely, if you fix the <term>Alkahest</term> <note>The hypothetical universal solvent</note> into a state of highest redness by itself, or join it immediately at the start with purified metallic Gold; and therein perfect the <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>. That this can be dissolved in all liquids for medicinal use is a belief that did not just begin today. For the Alkahest—in which (as was said above) lies hidden that soul of Nitre, which is also called the "Spiritual Hidden Sun and Moon" <note>original: *Sol & Luna*, the masculine/feminine or gold/silver principles</note>, the "Invisible Sulphur of Nature," and the "Living Gold of the Philosophers"—powerfully dissolves corporal metallic Gold. Then Gold, mixed with Gold within the nature of the subject, causes that spiritual Gold to vivify this corporal one; and this corporal Gold thickens that spiritual one. Thus the Spirit becomes Body, and the Body becomes Spirit, and both together become one spiritual and inseparable thing, etc.

If these things seem far-fetched: then can the <term>Sulphurs</term> <note>The "soul" or coloring principle of a metal</note> be extracted from metallic Gold with the help of crude purified Nitre (or more truly, Nitre regenerated in a peculiar way) in the space of a single hour and with almost no effort? Will this too, I ask, O Reader, seem so improbable? Much more so, I think. Nevertheless, the matter is as I have said: for I did not just experience this today or yesterday, but have tested it many times. Scarcely a week or two passes when, in the presence of several of my friends, I test and process each of the metals in order, so that their Sulphurs having been extracted from any one of them and cast separately into an open crucible, they were already potable after a few hours had passed. To these friends, among other things, I also show the purple Sulphur of "Luna" <note>Silver</note>, which is exactly like that which can be found in "Sol" <note>Gold</note>, or "Venus" <note>Copper</note>, or even "Mars" <note>Iron</note> itself. Thus, he who wished to commend himself to the literary world under the name *Philaletha* <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist whose identity remained a secret</note> wrote correctly: *Our Sun is in all common metals; but it is contained more closely in Gold and Silver.*

Therefore, oh happy, thrice happy is that Practitioner who, through the most merciful blessing of the supreme Jehovah, achieves the art of making and preparing that **SALT** which is, as it were, divine! By its effective operation, the metallic or mineral body is corrupted, destroyed, and dies; yet, in the meantime, its soul is brought back to life for the glorious resurrection of the Philosophical Body! # CHAPTER X.
## *On the Virtues of the Gold of the Air in the Animal Kingdom.*

For those who wish to preserve the vital Spirit in youths—which is the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>In medieval medicine, "radical moisture" was the innate fluid thought to be the fuel for the body's life-heat; its depletion led to old age and death</note> and heat, the <term>innate mummy</term> <note>A Paracelsian term referring to the life-force or "balsam" naturally present in the body that resists decay and heals wounds</note>, which has its seat in the center of a person's heart as the support of all our life—and for those who wish to restore that spirit when it languishes in the elderly, and to return them as if to youth in terms of strength, thus bringing the health of man to its highest peak: there will be a need for them to seek not an elemental heat, but that celestial heat of the **Sun** and **Moon**. This heat dwells in a more incorruptible substance found beneath the sphere of the Moon. One must make this heat similar to our own heat or Spirit, which occurs when it has been prepared into a medicine and a most sweet food; so that when taken by mouth, it immediately penetrates the human body, keeps every part (especially the flesh) united to itself and free from corruption, nourishes, increases, and restores the virtue and spirit of life, digests everything raw, prunes away every excess of whatever quality, makes the natural moisture abound, and ensures that the weak natural heat or fire is comforted, inflamed, and increased. *See Oswald Croll* <note>A German alchemist and professor of medicine, author of the highly influential *Basilica Chymica* (Royal Chemistry)</note> *in the Admonitory Preface to his "Royal Chemistry," page 101.*

*Petrus Poterius* <note>Pierre de la Poterie, a 17th-century French physician and alchemist</note> mentions a medicine of this kind in his *Consultations and Medical Advice for the Christian King, in the Spagyric Pharmacy, book 3, appendix, page 602.* He says: if you desire this universal medicine, ensure it is illuminated by the splendor of the **Sun**, by whose influence not only are its shadows removed, but by its benefit and heat, the compacted matter is thinned and truly fermented.

Just as this "Gold of the Air" of mine owes its birth and origin to the heat of the **Sun**, so if it is to be joined and fixed with the Spirit or **Salt of the World**—extracted not only from its own <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In alchemy, "Magnesia" refers not to the modern element, but to the "primal matter" or a universal receptive substance</note> but also from the rays of the **Moon** (and for that reason called *Lunaria* by the Philosophers)—who could doubt that the most excellent medicine for the world that ever was or will be is promised, and can indeed be made? *Compare Robert Fludd* <note>Robert Fludd (1574–1637) was a prominent English physician, alchemist, and mystic</note>.

# CHAPTER XI.

...also the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"; an alchemical preparation believed to capture the vital, restorative essence of the atmosphere</note>, by the benefit of which the tightness of the chest, difficulty in breathing, and other symptoms appeared so much relieved that the patient now enjoys the best of health.

In the month of June, I administered this Spirit to a pregnant woman suffering from difficulty in breathing arising from the upward pressure of the uterus, accompanied by an unnatural heat; once it was taken, her breathing soon became easier and the heat diminished.

A poor widow of this city still testifies that in the month of July, in a case of <term>hectic fever</term> <note>A remittent fever typically associated with "phthisis" or pulmonary tuberculosis</note> joined with <term>phthisis</term>, the Spirit of the World made her breathing easier.

To the three-year-old daughter of *Matthæus Hermann*, a citizen of this city, who had suffered from a long-lasting cough, we gave eight drops of the **Tincture of the Gold of the Air** <note>original: "tincturæ Auri Auræ"</note> in a little beer four times a day, specifically because she rejected other medicines; that night, she was no longer so violently tormented by that monstrous cough and she slept. Nor did we omit the use of this tincture in the following days, meeting with even better success.

In cases of <term>tertian fever</term> <note>A fever, often malarial, that returns every third day</note> in several individuals—specifically once the shivering had ended and the heat was beginning to take hold—I gave half a spoonful of the Spirit of the World to test its effectiveness. Soon after it was taken, the intense heat, which otherwise would have lasted for many hours, subsided.

The sixteen-year-old daughter of the Most Noble and Valiant Lord *à Milkauvv, Hereditary Lord in Merksdorff*, fell into an acute fever while traveling in the month of August, accompanied by a most vehement thirst, swelling of the tongue, intolerable burning of the throat, difficulty in breathing, delirium, and a total loss of strength. After the Spirit of the World was administered six times along with the Tincture of the Gold of the Air—not neglecting, in addition, <term>julaps</term> <note>Sweet, medicinal syrups</note> and <term>bezoardic powders</term> <note>Powders made from bezoars (animal stomach stones), used as universal antidotes and to strengthen the heart</note>—all the most dangerous symptoms subsided. And after her restoration to health, the most noble maiden confessed that the Spirit of the World had been the best medicine for defeating the disease.

An Illustrious Baroness, purely <term>scorbutic</term> <note>Suffering from scurvy, which in this period was often a "catch-all" diagnosis for various chronic pains and skin conditions</note> and frequently tormented by the symptoms of this evil—namely pain and redness of the head and eyes, palpitations of the heart, tension in the <term>hypochondria</term> <note>The upper abdomen; such tension was often linked to "melancholy" or digestive distress</note>, and a resulting anxious sweating of the palms of both hands—whenever she tasted half a spoonful of the Spirit of the World (even while pregnant), not only did the aforementioned symptoms and fantastical dreams cease, but she also passed her nights calmly, which she had otherwise spent in restlessness. On the third day after her childbirth, her <term>lochia</term> <note>The normal vaginal discharge of blood and tissue after giving birth</note> stopped, whence intense heat and thirst, night vigils, and frequently recurring <term>lipothymia</term> <note>Fainting or swooning</note> began to break her strength. # DE VIRTUTIBUS AURI.
<note>original: "On the Virtues of Gold"</note>

...[medications] helping, we finally granted a place to this Spirit and perceived a great relief of the mentioned symptoms, so that on the seventh day the <term>lochia</term> <note>postpartum discharge from the uterus</note> flowed properly again.

In cases of <term>serpigo</term> <note>a creeping skin disease like ringworm</note>, <term>impetigo</term>, or <term>lichen</term> <note>various forms of skin eruptions or rashes</note>, the **Spirit of the World** applied externally performs its duty by anointing; for it dries, removes current heat, and soothes the painful itching, and by doing this, it completes the entire work. This was found true by *Mr. Gabler, Pharmacist and Senator of Haynau* <note>modern Chojnów, Poland</note>, on his finger.

A respectable widow saw a miraculous effect in a <term>serpigo</term> of the lips from repeated anointing, after many other things had been tried elsewhere but applied here in vain.

And what it provided in these cases, I have experienced myself that it can also do in <term>scabies</term> <note>a contagious, itchy skin condition caused by mites</note>. For as often as I anointed a scabby hand several times day and night, I found that the pustules were so dried out in the following days that I could scratch them off without harm.

A leading citizen of *Haynau* at this time, who was a soldier in the *Hungarian* war, suffered from <term>Hungarian fever</term> <note>historically associated with typhus, common in military camps</note>; after the war ended, he returned to his fatherland not yet fully restored. After the use of medications, he was considered healthy. But after entering into marriage, he began to suffer from <term>podagra</term> <note>gout affecting the feet</note> and <term>chiragra</term> <note>gout affecting the hands</note>. Through various years he used various medications. In vain. At last, by our advice, he took refuge in the **Spirit of the World** and the **Tincture of the Gold of the Air** <note>original: "Tincturam Auri Auræ"</note>. The daily use of this not only restrained the immense pain during the time of the <term>paroxysm</term> <note>a sudden attack or intensification of symptoms</note> and soothed the otherwise more intense heat; but also every day he excreted through his urine small grains—red from the beginning, afterwards saffron-colored stones and white, viscous <term>tartareous</term> <note>referring to stony deposits believed to be caused by "tartar" in the body</note> matter—urinating more often and more copiously than had otherwise occurred in such quantity. Hence, I consider this Spirit with the Tincture as a <term>lithontriptic</term> <note>a medicine for dissolving or breaking down stones in the bladder or kidneys</note> diuretic not to be despised. And from that time since he has used this Spirit, the worst of evils—although it used to attack monthly as was its habit elsewhere—now knocks lightly at the door but has never been able to exert intolerable strength. I shall tell of wonders which will affirm that the **Spirit of the World** is <term>sudorific</term> <note>inducing perspiration</note>. At one time, I persuaded him to take a hot bath; before entering it, he took the aforementioned quantity of the **Spirit of the World** with the **Tincture of the Gold of the Air**; after leaving, white pustules the size of millet seeds appeared across the entire circumference of his body, full of water and breakable by light scratching. From these signs taken together, a great hope of restoration shines forth. We shall continue

### CHAPTER X.

...the daily use of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritûs Mundi"; in early modern medicine, this was believed to be a universal life-force or "world-soul" captured in liquid form</note> so that we may see what it is capable of; all of these findings we shall faithfully share with our own and future generations, God willing, by including these inquisitive observations.

A widow of our city, due to excessive grief, fell into a hectic-like fever on February 5th, with shivering and subsequent <term>phlogosis</term> <note>an ancient term for inflammation or burning heat</note> returning frequently during the day. Sleeplessness by night and day had so exhausted her for five days that she had no inclination toward sleep, and much less toward bowel movements. Her appetite was destroyed, and she was always thirsty. On the sixth day of the illness, the evil grew worse, especially toward evening, because of very troubling news. A supreme loss of strength and a fainting of the heart followed. She refused medicines, being desirous of death. On the eighth day of the illness, around ten o'clock in the morning, she was troubled by a fainting of the spirit, a darkening of the eyes, and a loss of hearing, reasoning, and speech, along with a most bitter headache. Convulsive movements, <term>uterine strangulation</term> <note>original: "strangulatio uteri"; a historical diagnosis where the uterus was thought to "rise up" and cause choking or fainting, often associated with grief or "hysteria"</note>, and pain in the hands and feet showed she was near death. 

Having been revived a little by **Bezoardic powders** <note>powders made from "bezoars," stony masses found in the stomachs of animals, then prized as universal antidotes</note> with **Castoreum** <note>a pungent secretion from beaver glands, used historically to treat "nervous" and "uterine" disorders</note> and other medicines necessary to defeat the disease, she took a **uterine clyster** <note>a medicinal wash or enema applied to the womb</note>. On the following night she began to feel a little better, as she did on the ninth day. On the tenth day, around ten o'clock, the aforementioned attacks returned. To root these out, I first administered **Spirit of Hartshorn** <note>original: "Spiritum C. C." (Cornu Cervi); an ammonia-based solution distilled from deer antlers</note> with essence of castoreum in a small drink of wine; it relented slightly, but soon the whole evil returned more vehemently. 

I then tested what the **Spirit of the World** with the **Balsam of the World** could do in such a desperate case. I provided a dose of one spoonful of the Spirit with six drops of the aforementioned Balsam, and the symptoms ceased. I repeated the said dose of medicine every hour, to the great relief of the patient and the wonder of those standing by. Around ten o'clock on the eleventh day, the enemy again invaded the walls of health with its former fury; but the Spirit and the Balsam, like defenders stronger than the enemy, put it to flight immediately, except for a weakness of the heart. On the 12th day, around three in the afternoon, the illness returned, but after a taste of this medicine, it soon departed. On the 13th day, it followed its morning pattern, as it did for the next four days, but having been defeated, as was said before, it turned its back and fled.

The efficacy of the Spirit and Balsam of the World will be commended by the following case. A <term>puerpera</term> <note>a woman who has recently given birth</note>, the wife of a highly deserving Deacon of this place, fell into an acute fever on the ninth day after childbirth, with remarkable shivering followed by most intense heat, headache, difficult breathing, and other symptoms. I gave one ounce of this Spirit with thirty drops of the **Balsam of the World** for...

### # CONCERNING THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.

...taken in four doses over a period of two hours: soon after, God be praised, we saw her restored.

A servant girl in the village of Walda (whom rumor said was pregnant) was confined to her bed by an acute fever on March 6th. She was sent nothing but one drachm of **Balsam of the World** <note>original: "Balsami Mundi"; an alchemical preparation believed to contain the "soul" of the world, often used as a panacea</note>, to be taken as twelve drops in a spoonful of warm beer every hour until four <term>scruples</term> <note>an apothecary weight equal to 20 grains or about 1.3 grams</note> were consumed. To lend greater credibility to the outcome, I have decided to include the very words of the Prefect to the Electress of Saxony, **Mr. Johann Martin Richter**: "Our servant girl was first seized by the disease with shivering and a subsequent significant heat; the symptoms were headache, <term>cardialgia</term> <note>historically, this referred to pain in the upper stomach or 'heart-burn,' often accompanied by anxiety</note>, difficult breathing, and piercing pains in the <term>hypochondria</term> <note>the upper abdominal region below the ribs</note>. After using the **Balsam of the World**, everything gradually subsided to such an extent that on the third day she was able to return to her usual duties."

The **Spirit of the World**, especially when combined with its **Balsam**, provides relief in desperate illnesses, acute fevers, or other such conditions that impede breathing and bring on an unnatural heat. If the body is first purged and freed from all superfluous filth and thickness, and if the dose given below is sipped, it brings relief, eases breathing, mitigates the 'flame' or internal burning, and restores lost strength in some way. If it does not do this immediately, I judge death to be near, as I have experienced in many cases so far. However, for those in whom there is hope of recovery, it helps immediately, as I saw happen only yesterday at nine o'clock in the evening: the wife of **Mr. Johann David Fischer** was plagued by a sudden constriction of the chest and difficult, suffocating breathing; her feet and arms, especially on the left side, were stiff and cold, so that she was thought to be having an **apoplectic fit** <note>a historical term for a stroke or sudden loss of consciousness and motion</note>. I gave six drachms of **Spirit of the World** with thirty drops of **Balsam of the World**, divided into four doses. After sipping the first dose, she felt relief and easier breathing; a little later she took the second, which was followed by sweat, and then sleep; in the morning I found her restored. August 28th, 1674. Thus far are the experiments of the aforementioned **Dr. Fritius**.

At my suggestion, the most renowned **Mr. Johann Engelhard**, a Candidate of Medicine and a friend most proven to me over many years, did the same and recorded in writing what he had observed. I thought it would be a useful thing to note those observations here as well:

On September 2nd, 1673, in the afternoon, **Mr. Emanuel Centgraff**, the Actuary of the Prefecture of Hayn, was suddenly afflicted by intolerable **griping pains** <note>original: "torminibus"; severe intestinal cramps or colic</note> of the bowels for three full hours, accompanied by shivering...

### CHAPTER X.

...the daily use of the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritûs Mundi"; a highly refined alchemical substance believed to contain the vital essence of the universe</note>, so that we may see what it is capable of. All these things, God willing, we shall faithfully share with both our own century and those to come, honestly including these curious observations.

A widow of our city, due to excessive grief, fell into a fever resembling a <term>hectic fever</term> <note>historically, a persistent fever often associated with wasting diseases, but here linked to emotional trauma</note> on February 5th, with shivering and subsequent <term>phlogosis</term> <note>original: "phlogoſi"; a term for inflammation or intense internal heat</note> returning frequently throughout the day. Sleeplessness by night and day had so exhausted her for five days that she had no inclination for sleep, and much less for any bowel movements. Her appetite was ruined, and she was always thirsty. On the sixth day of the illness, the malady grew worse, especially toward evening, on account of very distressing news. An extreme loss of strength and a fainting of the heart followed. She refused medicines, being desirous of death. On the eighth day of the illness, however, around ten o'clock in the morning, she was troubled by a fainting fit, a darkening of the eyes, and a loss of hearing, reasoning, and speech, along with the most bitter headache. Convulsive movements, <term>strangulation of the womb</term> <note>original: "ſtrangulatio uteri"; a historical diagnosis for what was then called "hysteria," believed to be caused by the womb displacing or constricting</note>, and pain in the hands and feet showed her to be near death. Having been roused a little, through **Bezoardic powders** <note>powders made from bezoars, stone-like masses found in the stomachs of animals, used as universal antidotes</note> with **Castoreum** <note>a secretion from beavers used to treat nervous conditions</note> and other medicines necessary to defeat the disease, she took a uterine <term>clyster</term> <note>a medicinal wash or enema</note>. During the following night she began to feel a little better, as she did on the ninth day. 

On the tenth day, around ten o'clock, the aforementioned attacks returned. To root these out, I applied from the start **Spirit of Hartshorn** <note>original: "Spiritum C. C." (Cornu Cervi); an aqueous solution of ammonia used as a stimulant</note> with essence of castoreum in a small drink of wine; the illness relented slightly, but soon the whole evil returned more vehemently. I decided to try what the **Spirit of the World** with the **Balsam of the World** could do in such a desperate case. I provided for a dose one spoonful of the Spirit with six drops of the aforementioned Balsam, and the symptoms ceased. I repeated the stated dose of medicine every hour, to the great relief of the patient and the wonder of those standing by. Around ten o'clock on the eleventh day, the enemy again invaded the walls of her health with a fury greater than before; but the Spirit and Balsam, like defenders stronger than the enemy, immediately put it to flight, except for a weakness of the heart. On the 12th day, around three in the afternoon, the illness returned, but after a taste of this medicine, it soon departed. On the 13th day, she observed a morning <term>type</term> <note>the recurring pattern or schedule of a fever</note>, as she did for the following four days, but being defeated as previously said, the disease turned its back and fled.

The following case will commend the efficacy of the **Spirit and Balsam of the World**. A woman who had recently given birth <note>original: "Puerpera"</note>, the wife of a well-deserving local Deacon, fell into an acute fever on the ninth day after delivery, with significant shivering and a subsequent most intense heat, headache, difficult breathing, and other symptoms. I gave one ounce of this Spirit with thirty drops of the **Balsam of the World**, for wha-

On March 6th, an acute fever confined a maidservant in the village of Walda (whom rumor said was pregnant) to her bed. After the use of the **Balsam of the World**, everything gradually subsided to such an extent that on the third day she was able to return to her usual duties.

If the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note>, especially when combined with its Balsam, is tasted in the dose given below—after the body has first been purged and freed from all dregs and superfluous thickness—it may bring relief in desperate diseases, acute fevers, or others of that kind that impede breathing and induce preternatural heat. It may ease respiration, mitigate the flame or fire <note>referring to the feverish heat</note>, and in some way restore lost strength. Otherwise, I judge death to be near, as I have experienced in many cases until now. However, in those for whom there is hope of recovery, it helps immediately, as I saw happen as recently as yesterday at nine o'clock in the evening: a sudden tightness of the chest and difficult, suffocating breathing troubled the wife of Mr. Johann David Fischer; her feet and arms, especially on the left side, were stiff and cold, so that she was thought to be <term>apoplectic</term> <note>suffering from a stroke or sudden loss of movement and sensation</note>. I gave six drachms of the **Spirit of the World** with thirty drops of the **Balsam of the World**, for four doses. After tasting the first dose, she perceived relief and freer breathing; a little later she swallowed the second; sweat followed, succeeded by sleep; in the morning, I found her restored. August 28, 1674. Thus far the experiments of the aforementioned ***Mr. Dr. Friebe***.

### CHAPTER X.

...thing, as well as suppression of urine and vomiting. I administered to him, in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"; in alchemy, this refers to a universal life-force or a highly refined solvent thought to contain the essence of nature</note>, six drops of the **Tincture of Golden Breeze** <note>original: "Tincturae Auri Aurae"</note>. A most pleasant desire for sleep then took hold of him, and after enjoying a quiet rest for about half an hour, he awoke feeling a lessening of his pains; he took food and felt very well. Rising in the morning to examine his urine, he found it greatly diluted and turbid, but the sediments poured out separately reflected a blood-red color, providing a clear testimony to the power of this <term>anti-nephritic</term> <note>kidney-healing</note> medicine.

On August 22nd of the current year, this same man was suddenly overcome by a feverish chill and shivering, which was immediately followed by extreme exhaustion of the limbs and a loss of strength. Forced to take to his bed, he appealed to the *Most Noble Mr. C. A. Balduin* <note>Christian Adolph Balduin (1632–1682), a noted alchemist and official</note>, *my greatest patron and benefactor*, to share some of his medicines. Mr. Balduin graciously granted his request, and I sent the man two doses of the **Spirit of the World**, into which had been infused several drops of the **Golden Breeze** and the **Balsam of the World**. After taking the first dose, he fell asleep instantly and rested peacefully for three full hours. When these had passed, he woke up feeling enormous headaches. This was undoubtedly due to the operation of the medicine itself, which was busy stirring up and ejecting the "Camarina" <note>An ancient Greek proverb "Do not move Camarina" referred to a stagnant marsh; medically, it means the medicine was agitating and clearing out the "swamp" of foul humors or waste clogging the body</note> with which his body was greatly filled. As proof of the outcome, his bowels evacuated themselves twenty times or more. From this, his strength was not a little broken, but through sleep—in which the patient spent almost the entire following day—he clearly labored to recover himself, and not without success. Indeed, after a day or two had passed, he returned to his former duties healthy and unharmed. Out of fear that it might weaken him by attacking his bowels as before, he decided to delay taking the second dose until the fourth day; however, nothing he feared happened, and he began to feel better every day thereafter.

This same gentleman's father, who was most miserably afflicted by a <term>bladder stone</term> <note>original: "calculo vesicae"</note> in the month of March, anxiously implored the help of the praised Author. Grieving for his plight, the Author allowed the **Spirit of the World** to be offered to him, mixed with its own **Balsam**. After a single dose was administered, and two or three hours had passed, he passed a stone slightly exceeding the size of a bean, followed shortly after by several smaller ones. Once these were expelled, he was restored to his former health by the grace of God.

### ON THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.

...so much so that even after applying the most noble medicines, her life was despaired of in vain. When this flow <note>original: "fluxus"; likely a uterine hemorrhage or severe bleeding</note> had persisted into the third day and beyond, it happened that the **Noble Mr. Balduin** met with the host of this elderly woman, **Mr. Andreas Uschner**, who serves as the **Secretary of Records and Letters for the Senate of Haynau**. Among other things, Uschner mentioned her desperate state, asserting that nothing but imminent death remained and was indeed fast approaching. I, who was present at this conversation, hearing these things, said: "What if some of the **Balsam of the World** <note>original: "Balsamô Mundi"; a highly refined alchemical oil</note> were administered to her? It can do little harm if it brings no relief." Mr. Balduin agreed and ordered several doses to be given to her. After I had administered these in succession, the hemorrhage began to subside; and although this ailment returned more than once, her strength—which she previously could not exert at all—eventually prevailed. Leaving the palm of victory to this most noble medicine, the illness was compelled to retreat entirely. By God's merciful blessing and contrary to everyone's expectation, the aforementioned woman remains a healthy survivor to this day.

Finally, the **Noble Mr. Balduin** wished to conduct an experiment upon himself regarding the remarkable and never-sufficiently-praised virtue of the **Balsam of the World** in cases of <term>odontalgia</term> <note>toothache</note>. Last winter, while suffering most severely from his teeth, he happened upon the Balsam of the World and decided to test whether it had any power against this condition. He prepared a gargle for himself from it, and upon applying it to the affected part, he felt immediate relief. For the pains, though they were quite violent at first, vanished completely beyond all hope after the passage of a single hour, and they have not returned since. 

->*This ends the Observations of Engelhard.*<-

I would have you observe here that the origin and "cradle" of both the **Golden Breeze** <note>original: "Auri Auræ"</note> and the **Balsam of the World** are, as it were, the **Spirit of the World**. Therefore, as it is prepared for Medicine, this "Spirit of the World" is nothing other than a simple and subtle <term>quintessence</term> <note>the "fifth essence," the purest possible extract</note> separated from the body of <term>Gold</term> <note>symbol in original: ☉</note>—treating the metal as the thick and coarse matter—and separated from the superfluities of the four Elements. You will see what prevents me from calling it the "General Physical Stone" and the "Elixir composed by Nature" (drawing from the *Treatise on the True Secret Salt* by the philosopher **Mr. Nuysement**, chapter 4), by means of which Nature performs all her miracles. It is much more worthy of admiration than the "Stone of the Chemists," to which it is granted—only through this very Spirit—to act upon its like in order to introduce that which is lacking.

This Spirit of the World, which I have mentioned so often, is an entirely penetrating and volatile essence with a most sweet odor, possessing a taste like that of sweet almonds. If one were to swallow even a few small drops, it can scarcely be described...

For these very things, may the most Glorious Divinity ensure that we are never ungrateful!

### CHAPTER XI.

->*On the Virtues of the Golden Breeze in the Mineral Kingdom.*<-

The duties of life should not be disrupted, nor should those things proper to adults—whom a firm age has already made men—be imposed upon the weak age of children; as the Prince of Poets <note>likely a reference to Virgil or Horace</note> might allow me to borrow that sentiment here. Mindful of this precept,

1. I do not attribute to this **Golden Breeze** of the higher [world], which is like my own offspring, those things which belong to the robust and to those who can do more than children. For it has not yet exceeded, as it were, its years of infancy. But if it should advance beyond its strength, then at last it will be possible to hope well and trust most highly in it. Since, however, it is a Solar **Sulfur** <term>Sulfur</term> <note>In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, fiery principle of a substance</note>; and it is generally admitted among Philosophers, I believe, that all **Sulfurs** of Tincture—specifically those fixed and united with other metals—now leave me no room for doubt; that this **Golden Breeze** will surely have more power over other imperfect metals (once its own perfection and fermentation with metallic Gold is established) than those vegetable and mineral sulfurs can achieve. Yet it must be held again and again: in this stupendous work, the primary honors must be given and deferred entirely to the most universal volatile **Salt** <term>Salt</term> <note>The third alchemical principle, representing the body or solid base of a substance</note>.

# ON THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.

Which you will obtain very easily from Saltpeter <note>original: "Nitro"</note>, if you recall those things which were presented above; so I would also wish you to observe the method of preparation by the example through which Tartar <note>potassium bitartrate, a deposit found in wine barrels</note> is made volatile. While this process has tormented more than a few; among them *Jan Baptist van Helmont in his Book on Fevers, Ludovicus de Comitibus in discussion 6, page 38 of my copy, and Daniel Ludovici in his Dissertation on Volatile Tartar, discussion 9, page 66*; there have, on the contrary, been those who considered that they had to labor hardly at all here. You may consult, if you please, *Dr. Joel Langelott in his Letter to the Excellent Seekers of Nature, pages 5 and 10, Dr. David von der Becke in his Letter to Langelott, and Dr. Georg Wolfgang Wedel in his Specimen of Chemical Experiments, page 55.* Also others. The same *von der Becke in his Experiments and Meditations concerning the Principles of Natural Things, page 130 and following.*

Furthermore, a few weeks ago, I found a much easier way by which not only Tartar and Vitriol <note>sulfate minerals</note> and common Salt, and other things of this kind, but even Saltpeter <note>original: "Nitrum"</note> itself, in a very few hours, may be distilled into the Spirit of the World <note>original: "Mundi Spiritum"</note>, or a water of quite pleasant taste, by the benefit of a glass alembic <note>a pear-shaped distilling vessel used in chemistry</note>, and thus further transformed into a volatile white Salt. From this, the true **Alkahest** <note>the fabled universal solvent</note> (by the benefit of the concentrated <term>Spirit of Salt 🜔</term> <note>original symbol 🜔; hydrochloric acid</note>, about which I spoke above) comes forth. What is more? In itself, it can even serve as a most immediate remedy for driving away burning fevers. But of that, I speak only in passing here.

Thus, secondly, we trust that the lower Hermetic Gold of the lower Breeze <note>original: "Auræ inferioris Hermeticum Aurum"; a wordplay on "Aurum" (gold) and "Aura" (breeze)</note> can do more in the mineral kingdom. For not only did *Dr. Petrus Johannes Faber, in his Panchymicum, book 40, chapter 4, write things scarcely believable about Saltpeter: namely, that in the innermost bowels of nature is contained a closed fire of life; if someone could coagulate it, make it fixed, and give it a golden or silver tincture <note>color or essence</note>, he would possess the Secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note>; but also through Saltpeter, Gansandus both began his work and successfully put the finishing touch upon it.*

Just as we do not doubt, therefore, that the Stone <note>the Philosopher's Stone</note> is prepared from the saltpeter-like liquor of the **Alkahest** (either by itself or joined with common Gold); so if you wish to transform imperfect metals <note>base metals like lead or tin</note>, you must project <note>add or throw</note> the Elixir upon <term>Gold ☉</term> melted in a crucible <note>original: "tigillo"</note>; and thus that Salt, having entered the gold, is finally changed into a brittle Salt, yet one that is soluble and meltable with the slightest heat. Which indeed (as I believe), while it is impregnated with Gold and projected upon imperfect metals, transmutes them into Gold.

### CHAPTER XI.

...[the metals] themselves were made golden. This method is attributed to Glauber <note>Johann Rudolph Glauber (1604–1670), a famous German-Dutch alchemist and chemist known for "Glauber's salt."</note> (in his *Purgation of Philosophy*, page 38 of my copy), which you may consult if you are so inclined. Hence, although **Bartholinus** (in his *Specimen of Physics*, page 4, chapter 2, published at Florence) testifies that he saw among the precious things of the **Duke of Tuscany** <note>The Medici family, who were famous patrons of the sciences and alchemy.</note> a continuous nail, half of which was gold and half iron—the part that had been immersed in the tincture being gold—and it was said to have been transmuted long ago by **Thurneysser** <note>Leonhard Thurneysser (1531–1596), a Swiss goldsmith and alchemist known for his public demonstrations of "transmuting" iron nails into gold by dipping them into a secret liquid.</note>; yet there are others, in turn, who, regarding it as a fraud discovered after that time, plainly reject and dismiss the claim.

The most noble **Lord Augustus Heyland** visited me not so long ago. Our conversation was varied—not only concerning French, Italian, and English matters, since he had not just briefly looked at those lands, but other things as well. At last, as he is curious about every work of Nature, we conveniently fell into the subject of **Chemistry**. He mentioned a small penknife <note>original: "Ensiculus," a tiny blade used for sharpening quill pens.</note> that he kept; he said it was a gift from a chemist of great name while he was in **Venice**. In all other respects, the edge and the core were iron, but the tip was gold. Those who had seen it believed it had been changed into Gold by the power and efficacy of some tincture. 

I confess that a desire to see such a rare gift immediately entered my mind, and I began to ask him so persistently that he did not mind sending it to me. He agreed, however, on the condition that I should indicate in a letter what I thought of it when I returned it, which I promised to do. Therefore, as soon as the opportunity was given to me to examine it, I could not wonder at it enough. The tip was equal in length to half a finger joint. It was gold of the quality usually found in gold crowns, and when tested against a touchstone <note>original: "Lydium lapidem," a dark stone used by jewelers to test the purity of precious metals by the color of the streak they leave.</note>, it responded exactly. Finally, there was not even a single sign of soldering, so that it was neither possible to state plainly that it was the result of a Tincture, nor yet to guess at the art and combination used. 

However, there was a reason why I inclined toward a particular suspicion. Without delay, I took a knife, broke off the point, and arranged for another tip to be welded <note>original: "ferruminandum," meaning to join or solder together</note> from gold. When the file of a goldsmith (for I had given him that task) had been applied to it, either I am remarkably mistaken, or the work emerged not a little more ornate and polished than the original. Certainly it seemed at the very least equal to that **Venetian** one; the entire labor took five hours. Do not believe that I am making anything up here. For iron or steel loves—or rather, with the right heat, greedily attracts—Gold. (*By which experiment the opinion of **Otto Tachenius** in his "Key to Ancient Hippocratic Medicine," chapter 8, is refuted—namely, that Gold cannot be joined to iron before it takes on a copper-like nature.*) Thus art is perpetually conquered by Art; nor was the fraud so ingenious that the labor involved did not equally reveal and expose the trick.

Meanwhile, this remains: that the Steel, which we have spoken of so far, and other...

### CONCERNING THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.

...that imperfect metals <note>In alchemy, "imperfect" or "base" metals like lead, tin, or iron were thought to be "diseased" versions of the "perfect" gold.</note> can most truly be transmuted into Gold by the benefit of the Philosopher's Stone (provided that it can be obtained).

However, as for he who—along with **Thomas Moresinus** of Aberdeen, the Scot (in *On the Causes and Transubstantiation of Metals*), **Nicolaus Guibertus** of Lorraine, and also **Father Athanasius Kircher** <note>A famous Jesuit polymath who was highly skeptical of alchemists’ claims to make real gold.</note> (in his *Subterranean World*, Volume 2, book 11, section 2, chapter 11)—roundly denies that the preparation of the "Philosophers' Tincture" <term>Tincture: a potent alchemical substance, often a powder or liquid, used to transform base metals.</term> is possible by purely natural art, which might substantially tinge all metals into Gold and Silver and multiply itself through "projection" <term>Projection: the process of casting a small amount of the alchemical catalyst onto a large mass of molten base metal to transform it.</term> from a finite amount into infinity; let that person read the *Peripatetic Spear* by **Valeriano Bonvicino**, Professor at Padua; the *Brief Interpellations* of **Salomon de Blauenstein**; and especially the *Spagyric Appendix to the Royal Pharmacopoeia* by **Johann Zwelfer** (part 1, chapter 1) and what he built up there against the said **Kircher** in favor of the truth and possibility of *Chrysopoeia* <term>Chrysopoeia: the Greek term for the art of gold-making.</term>. 

Let him also read **Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a medieval scholar often cited as an alchemical authority.</note> in his 30th book *On Minerals*; the treatise of **Master Petrus Bonus** of Lombardy from Ferrara, composed in the year 1330; **Heinrich Salmuth** (book 2, title 2, page 340); **Giovanni Francesco Pico della Mirandola** *On Gold* (book 3, chapter 2); **Marsilio Ficino** *On the Celestial Life* (book 2, chapter 3); **Dr. Adam von Bodenstein’s** *Introduction to Arnald of Villanova’s Rosary*, in the Epistle prefixed to the Fuggers from Basel (page 46 of my copy); and **Bernard Penot** of Port-Sainte-Marie in his *Apologetic Preface against Guibert*, who in his own time saw at Prague, in the house of **Dr. Thaddaeus** <note>Thaddaeus von Hayeck, personal physician to Emperor Rudolf II.</note>, an entire pound of quicksilver <note>Mercury.</note> converted into the purest Gold by **Edward Kelley** <note>An English medium and alchemist famous for his partnership with John Dee; he claimed to have found a red powder that could transmute mercury.</note> using only a single small drop of a most red liquid. 

One should also consult **Fernelius** *On the Hidden Causes of Things* (book 2, chapter 18); **Dr. Johann Wolfgang Dienheim** *On Universal Medicine* (chapters 24 and 25); **Matthias Unzer’s** *Spagyric Anatomy of Mercury* (book 1, chapter 18); **Master Georg Froberg’s** *History of Alchemy* (page 78); **Dr. Michael Maier** in his *Golden Symbolism of the Twelve Nations* (pages 219, 471, 480, 523, and 557); and **Jan Baptist van Helmont**, who in his writings publicly testifies three times that he performed a projection (pages 743, 793, and 671); as well as the *Curious Miscellany of Medical-Physical Matters* from the Academy of Nature Curiosities (Year 1, Observation 16); **Dr. Johann Tackius’s** *Animal and Mineral Birth of Gold* (page 3); and **Dr. Daniel Georg Morhof’s** *Epistle to Dr. Langelott on the Transmutation of Metals* (page 87 and following).

Although very few obtain this Tincture, it is by no means fitting to banish it from the nature of things. For it has—just like the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil of our First Parents—its own guardian spirit from heaven to protect it with a double-edged sword <note>original: "rhomphaea bis acuta." A reference to the flaming sword guarding Eden.</note> and to keep away the curious and the unworthy; for there are few who have learned how to join virtue with flowing riches.

Concerning this Medicine, Nature herself is introduced in a *Complaint* by an **ancient French author**:

> *It heals every malady,*
> *And he who has it never begs:*
> *He who has but an ounce and a single grain...*

# CHAPTER XII.

## *On the virtues of the Ethereal Gold in the Vegetable Kingdom.*

The author **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636) was a famous Polish alchemist who proposed that a vital "nitre" or life-force existed in the air.</note> states in his *Epilogue to the Treatises* that where the Solar rays strike more abundantly, a greater quantity of the Salt of Nitre is produced, and as a consequence, a greater abundance of grain grows. This is most true, if anything ever was. For plants receive their nourishment not only from the earth or from water, but from a certain something—specifically, a nitrous salt. 

Come, let us call upon experience, by which alone we are best taught. Do we not find that the more salt a field has, the more fertile it is? To give an example: because there is a great deal of Nitre <term>Nitre: In early modern science, this was not just saltpeter, but a "universal salt" or spiritual fertilizer believed to be pulled from the air by the sun's rays.</term> in the excrement of animals, manure makes the fields joyful and fat. Indeed, human urine itself is nothing other than pure Nitre (though not "animated" or living), according to the teachings of **Theophrastus Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss physician and alchemist who revolutionized medicine in the 16th century.</note> in his *First Treatise on the Plague* (page 376); thus, from the earth where cattle urinate, the Salt of Nitre is produced and "cooked" by the sun. As he also says in his *Fragments of Treatises* (page 449), we are easily led to believe that the "secret" held by some for multiplying the harvest is a particular lye or wash made from Nitre. 

<note>See the *Collection of Memoirs and Conferences on the Arts and Sciences presented to the Lord Dauphin during the year 1672*, by Jean-Baptiste Denis, Counselor and Physician in Ordinary to the King. *First Conference Regarding the Vegetation of Plants*, page 216.</note>

Certainly, the common opinion of the Philosophers holds that by using their "most universal matter," sterile trees can be made fertile, and herbs, flowers, and roots can be produced in the middle of winter. It is said that any tree can be made to yield mature fruit four times a year or more, that a tender shoot can be raised even from dried wood, and that even the most bitter fruits can be made sweet. This is reported by **Johann Walch** in his *German Philosophical and Chemical Treatise* (page 252).

**Albertus Magnus** <note>A 13th-century Dominican friar and polymath who was legendary for his supposed magical and alchemical powers.</note> obtained the name of "Mage" because, in the very coldest part of Midwinter, he was able to bring all kinds of fruit to full and perfect ripeness by the power of this "Ethereal Balsam" <term>Balsam: A refined alchemical substance thought to preserve life and promote growth.</term>, as noted by **Sir Kenelm Digby** <note>A 17th-century English courtier and natural philosopher known for his "powder of sympathy" and botanical experiments.</note> in his *Dissertation on the Vegetation of Plants* (page 72).

For our part, we do not overturn the faith of these words; nor is it easy for anyone to doubt that the "Universal Mercury" <term>Universal Mercury: The fundamental liquid or spiritual principle of all matter, believed by alchemists to be the key to growth and transformation.</term> provides some increase in growth and scent to any flower or plant; yet, when it is united with

->### ON THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.<-

That the Gold of the upper Atmosphere <note>original: "Auro Auræ superioris." In alchemical theory, this refers to a celestial, spiritual influence captured from the air.</note>, fixed into a stone for our use, acquires for itself even greater powers, is something I cannot doubt.

But what of the most perfect gold of the lower Atmosphere, that is, the Philosopher's Stone itself prepared from the Nitrous Alkahest <term>Alkahest: A legendary "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any solid body into its primary liquid form.</term>? Must we not admit that, before the fermentation of Gold, it possesses even greater power in the Vegetable Kingdom? Who could deny it? For, if we listen to **Walchius** again (p. 253), a Tincture is made from this Stone; if you dissolve a quantity the size of a millet seed in water, and a vine—specifically its "heart" or center—is later infused with it, it will not only immediately put forth branches, leaves, and flowers against the common course of nature, but—what will seem marvelous to all—it will also push out noble and ripe fruits. *See Paracelsus in "The Hermetic Apocalypse on the Elixir of Life."*

This brings to my memory that Secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> of resuscitated plants, by which they claim that plants already turned to ash and effectively dead can be called back to life—animated by heat as if by the divine wand of Mercury—and clothed in a new form. The first to bring this Secret onto the public stage, as far as I know, was **Quercetanus** <note>Joseph Duchesne (c. 1544–1609), a French physician who explored the chemical "signatures" of plants.</note>, who was shown it by a Polish physician, though the secret was not actually shared with him. Since then, several have mentioned it; but I do not recall anyone who gave a public description of it besides **Rosenbergius** in *Rhodologia* (though he covered it all in an obscure veil of words) and **Spontoni**, an Italian, in his *Treatise on the Viper*—but he did so in the same, or rather more obscure, manner as Rosenbergius. I would certainly believe that they never personally experienced the thing, but reported what they wrote either from their own imagination or from the unreliable communication of others. Hence **Billichius** and **Georgius Kirstienius**—the latter in his *German Adversaria* against **Johann Agricola**—denied all credit and assent to the whole business.

On the other hand, the Roman physician **Petrus Servius** witnessed a Rose born from burnt ashes, cultivated and labored over by a skillful and ingenious art, reaching full maturity and perfection within twenty-four hours (*Treatise on the Wonders of Nature and Art*).

From the speech of the English **Count Digby** <note>Sir Kenelm Digby (1603–1665), a famous natural philosopher and courtier.</note> *On the Vegetation of Plants*, we have word that **Father Athanasius Kircher** <note>A famous Jesuit polymath known for his massive encyclopedic works on the natural world and hidden mysteries.</note> performed the miracle of restoration and shared the method of operating with Digby. Yet Kircher seems to have begun this resuscitation not from the ashes themselves, but from the seed of the plants; indeed, we find him describing his process for the "Regermination of Plants" from the seed of any species. Look for what the same author says in *The Subterranean World*, Book 2, section 4, chapter 5, pages 413 and 414. And also what **Father Caspar Schott** says in *Hydraulic-Pneumatic Mechanics*, part 2, class 1, Machine 8. Although, therefore, Lord...

-># CHAPTER XII.<-

**Pierre Borel** writes in his *History and Medico-Physical Observations*, Observation 62: *I would believe these things possible if the plants are calcined in a closed vessel so that the **volatile salt**, in which the **resurrective power** <note>original: "vis resurrectiva"</note> lies hidden, does not evaporate; then I believe it must be collected and mixed with **fixed salt** by means of the finest **water of life** <note>original: "aquæ vitæ," usually referring to concentrated alcohol or a potent solvent</note> or **May dew**. These should then be **circulated** <term>Circulated: An alchemical process of heating a liquid in a sealed vessel so it repeatedly evaporates and condenses, meant to "purify" the substance.</term> for some time in a sealed vessel with moderate heat, so that the purified principles are properly united. I must confess, however, that I have never attempted these things myself; nonetheless, in place of May dew, I consider the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spirit of the World: A universal life force or "aether" that alchemists believed could be captured from the air to animate matter.</term> to be more suitable and effective for this work. I have stated the reasons for this above.*

The observations of **Master Pierre-Jean Fabre** in the first chapter of his *Spagyric Palladium* <note>original: "Palladio Spagyrico." Spagyric chemistry involves the separation and recombination of plant components.</note> also deserve notice. He claims that by using the "radical moisture" of the world, impregnated with light, flowers and plants can be planted and nourished in glass vials to grow and sprout. He suggests that trees and plants from distant lands can be transported safely and without decay, and can even sprout, flower, and bear fruit four times a year, provided the external cold of the surrounding air does not hinder or delay the ripening of the fruit; and that things half-dead can be resuscitated, or rather, renewed. However, there are those who distrust the author's experience here, and indeed wish it to be entirely dismissed. Nevertheless, I undertook this labor myself this past year and did not shrink from it. Meanwhile, I do not wish to cheat **Borel** of his due praise, for he pointed the way as if with a finger in his *History and Medico-Physical Observations*. In Observation 37, he says: *I would scarcely have believed that plants could be nourished and increased by water alone, had I not seen and kept a sprig of **basil** <note>original: "Ocymi & Basilici"</note> for six months in a vial full of water, where it put forth many—indeed, innumerable—roots, shoots, and flowers; but the water must be renewed often.* I have also observed trees of considerable size, even equaling the thickness of a shin-bone, thriving in a small earthen pot by watering alone, while the weight of the soil remained the same. Therefore, it is to be believed that they take their nourishment from the air and water.

**Pierre Potier** observed the same thing along with the most noble **Robert Boyle** while he was staying at the **English Academy at Oxford**. Sprigs of mint, pennyroyal, and lemon balm, when properly placed in glass vials filled with spring water that was never changed, unfolded into numerous roots and luxuriant leaves. See his *Preface to Medical and Chemical Works*, page 9. These praised authors undoubtedly sought this from **Paracelsus**, *On Transmutations*, Book 3, *On Conservation*, page 17.

**Libavius**, in his *Collection of Alchemical Secrets*, Book 1, Chapter 22 <note>original: "Syntagm. Arcan. Chym."</note>, recounts the history of a plant sprouting under the water itself in a glass, based on the report of another physician; he even includes a drawing of it there. **Frey** relates the same thing in **Rosenberg’s** *Rose-Knowledge* <note>original: "Rhodologia"</note>, asserting that he saw the same thing happen with a tulip; as does **Master Jacob.**
