# ON THE VIRTUES OF GOLD.

**Jacobus Dobrzenski von Schwarzpont** <note>A Bohemian physician and professor (1623–1693)</note>, in his *Pleasant Philosophy*, Part 1, Preface 1, discusses the sources of these wonders. But an even more admirable account is mentioned by a **French author** in a French treatise called *The Little Surgery* <note>original: "La petite Chirurgie," a work often attributed to Paracelsus or his followers</note>, Chapter 22; he describes a small glass vessel in which the entire world—adorned with trees, plants, flowers, meadows, rivers, mountains, and animals—can be viewed. However, I believe this phenomenon is of the same kind that the excellent **Johann Tacke** <note>German physician and alchemist (1589–1675)</note> claims to have once displayed in his *Mystery of the Resurrection of Things*, page 65 and following.

I observed this same vegetative growth in the **Magnet** <term>Universal Magnet: a substance alchemists believed could attract the "vital spirit" from the air</term> which I had designated as the "Universal" one this very year. For on the nineteenth day of March, when it had shown itself in round circles, and the air was drier around noon due to the solar rays, this Magnet of mine was seen to be somewhat transformed into an oil. Because this oil was very thick, it soon began to congeal and take on the forms of little trees and shrubs (a most pleasant sight to the eyes).

Since I knew well enough from my own experience—and had perceived abundantly from mechanical principles—that the **Spirit** <note>The "Spiritus Mundi" or world-soul, thought to be a life-giving force</note> is not only plentiful in the elements of air and water alike, but actually thrives more there than in other elements, I concluded the following: **Salt** is contained in air and water in both crystalline fixed and volatile forms. Indeed, flowers such as tulips, anemones, lilies, etc., can sprout with wonderful fertility and brilliance from a **lye** <note>A liquid solution made from ashes</note> made from the stalks and stems of those same plants after they have been burned to ash, if this solution is poured over the roots of the appropriate plants. Sinking deeper into thought on this matter, I concluded that if it were possible for water—which **Philipp Jakob Sachs von Löwenheim** <note>German physician (1627–1672)</note> calls the "solvent of the world" <note>original: "menstruum mundi"</note> in his *Macro-microcosmic Ocean*, page 26—to be increased with the **Universal Salt** <note>original: "Sale Catholico"</note> or the solidified Spirit of the World, its power for preservation would be much stronger.

For the Universal Salt is the **balsam** <note>A preservative or healing agent</note> of water. If water is not imbued with its own fire, it will not make plants very fruitful; therefore, something is necessarily contained within it for which the water serves only as a vehicle, and this is nothing other than the Salt that grants fertility to all things. **Sir Kenelm Digby** <note>English courtier and natural philosopher (1603–1665)</note> teaches in *On the Vegetation of Plants*, page 62, that not only all vegetables but even all minerals owe their origin to this Salt (when rightly known or understood). Indeed, **Domenico Panaroli** <note>Italian physician (1587–1657)</note> in his *Arcana*, volume II, page 218, strives to show that all things arise from water and fire, just as from sulfur and mercury, and thus that the seed is that life-giving force—composed of fire and water—residing in the sperm.

Leaning upon these foundations, I have successfully approached the task itself, and I have learned to preserve flowers and herbs by the benefit of the **fixed balsam of universal nature** combined with **vegetable** <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol in original: △</note>. Lest you think I am professing this more boastfully than truly, behold for yourself these specimens of various sizes...

...resting upon pillars, glass globes which I filled this past year with a manifold variety of flowers. These may be seen at my home at any time one wishes, providing no small delight to the eyes and soul. If God, the Best and Greatest, grants me strength and life, the coming year will establish a **glass garden** in our Museum. It will display to students of these matters violets, roses, narcissus, tulips, carnations, and other plants of that kind—as rare in their use as they are second to none in the brilliance of their colors. And this shall be possible regardless of the season of the year. 

I have not yet seen anyone who has achieved the like. Indeed, I am unlikely to see anyone who could perform this, unless they have inspected our *Flora* <note>The author's botanical/alchemical study</note>, which, at the request of many friends of no small reputation, is titled **EVER-LIVING** <note>original: "SEMPER-VIVA"</note> and will perhaps soon see the light of public publication.

Indeed, I see that several delays are being thrown in my way; but while these may postpone such matters, they cannot take away my promises.

But you, benevolent Reader, whoever you are, if anything in this Treatise of mine is not fully perceived or understood at first glance: I ask that you suspend your judgment on those things which are not clear to you; and concerning those things which seem clear enough, do not pronounce anything rashly. In an occult matter, do not offer any judgment other than that which you would wish a good man to apply to your own reputation.

Meanwhile, he has not yet adored the Majesty of **Nature naturing** <term>Natura naturans: a philosophical term for God as the creative force and source of all existence</term>, nor has he sufficiently yielded to **Nature natured** <term>Natura naturata: the created world and the physical laws that govern it</term>, who does not believe in the possibility of the divine **Hermetic work** <note>Alchemy, named after Hermes Trismegistus</note>, or who ignores or despises its reality. *Hurin of Parma, Paris.*

> For this was once very truly put forward by **Seneca** <note>Roman Stoic philosopher, known for his wisdom on human progress</note>: "Those who were before us have done much, but they have not finished the task; much work still remains, and much will remain; nor will the opportunity of adding something more be denied to any person born after many centuries."

# EPILOGUE
## to the Kind Reader.

Many people cannot easily restrain their minds, being inclined toward their own desires; they condemn even good things so that, being thus deterred, they may cease to take risks, and so fall less into worse errors. Looking at these things, there is no doubt that there will be—if not many, then at least some—who, perhaps not with bad intentions, will attack our opinion concerning the most universal **Matter of the Stone**. This is especially true of those who are enamored with some other subject—such as Antimony, Bismuth, native Cinnabar, **red silver ore** <note>original: "Rothgülden Erz"</note>, or whatever it may be—and who (as they say) hold onto it tooth and nail. They are perhaps moved by the authorities and examples of modern writers: by the most ingenious *Philalethes* <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist</note>, and the most curious *Becher* <note>Johann Joachim Becher, a German physician and alchemist</note>, for whom the "animated Mercury of Antimony" fills both sides of the page. What if the most noble **Author of the Tomb of Semiramis** <note>A reference to a popular 17th-century alchemical work, "Tumba Semiramidis"</note> himself, giving the highest honor to the distilled mercurial salt, should rouse them? However it may be, we thrust our opinion on no one. Nor do we wish it to be an article of faith. Let them believe and feel what they wish; let it be entirely up to them. We only ask that they observe and take care to weigh those conditions of the "subject of the Stone" which one may read scattered throughout the books of the most ancient Philosophers, to see whether they could be applied either to some running Mercury of Antimony, or to other specific materials of that kind. I shall not be wordy in recounting such things, which I noted down long ago.

>> I will give a few: (1.) It is one thing, of any individual thing, having no likeness to itself. (2.) Natural, simple. (3.) Of the first matter. (4.) Universal <note>original: "Catholica"</note>, not yet vegetable, nor animal, nor mineral. (5.) Vegetable, animal, and mineral at the same time, sufficiently filled with **Mercury**, **Sulfur**, and **Salt**. (6.) A chaos or chaotic. (7.) Corporeal nonetheless, and therefore (8.) visible. (9.) Adamic. (10.) Living in its own way. (11.) Perfect in itself. (12.) Having all things in itself, and needing nothing foreign. (13.) Most ancient, or truly Saturnine, *adrop* <term>Adrop: an alchemical term usually referring to the "Lead of the Sages" or the raw material of the Stone</term>, or the **Lead of the Wise**. (14.) Of which, according to the **Emerald Tablet of Hermes** <note>original: "tabulam Hermetis"</note>, the father is the **Sun**, the mother the **Moon**, and the nurse is the **Earth**. (15.) Always and everywhere findable, but

> ...not everywhere suitable for the craftsman. (16.) Cheap in the eyes of the common folk, but precious in the sight of the Wise. (17.) Known to all, recognized by very few. (18.) Sold freely and for nothing by God and nature. (19.) Strongly sealed. (20.) Weighty. (21.) Composed of water, congealed, resisting fire, not wetting the hands, permanent, the **ice of the wise** <note>original: "glacies sophorum"</note>. (22.) The Stone that is no-Stone. (23.) Raw philosophical Mercury <note>The symbol ☿ represents Mercury or Quicksilver</note>. (24.) Shining in the darkness. (25.) Our Macrocosmic Microcosm <note>The idea that the "small world" of the alchemical vessel reflects the "large world" of the universe</note>. (26.) From the seed of the Greater World, a Son of the same substance <note>original Greek: "ὁμοούσιος" (homoousios), a term borrowed from theology meaning "of the same essence," implying the Stone is made of the same "stuff" as the universe itself</note> as its parent, in whom is all the fullness of Nature, that is, the great **ELIXIR**, etc.

From this, many true Philosophers exclude all minerals and metallic bodies, all types of salts—except (in the words of Lully <note>Ramon Llull, a 13th-century philosopher often cited in alchemical texts</note>) the salt of our **Lunaria** <term>Lunaria: Literally "Moon-plant," but here an alchemical code for a specific silver-like substance</term>—and all vegetables and all animals. However, just as there is no small distinction between the "Greater Universal" and the "Lesser," or between the Philosopher's Stone and Philosophical Tinctures (for example, those of the sun [gold] or the moon [silver], even if they are generated from one seed and one mother), as is found in the works of *Basil* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary alchemical author</note> and others: I would not deny that you act rightly regarding a Tincture alone, if you select that subject which is not lacking in a Mercury specifically concentrated for the Tinctures of Metals. Such a thing, indeed, is **Antimony** (which I mentioned myself above, in Chapter III). And I can cite many adept Philosophers who entered upon the same path for making the Tincture—that plain and direct way—nor did they wander from the truth here because they employed the Mercury of Antimony, animated by the **sulfur of Mars** <note>iron</note> (the "fire of Metals") and fixed with Gold. And from this, a neglect of that most universal matter seems to have flowed; on the other hand, Antimony was rejected by those who, while not being ignorant of the "Universal" matter, viewed it only as a specific material.

Indeed, I do not deny that all metals have only one and the same essential form and matter, consisting in the cooking and perfection of **Quicksilver** <note>original: "Argenti vivi"</note> as the proximate matter of Metals; from which it can be gathered that the tincture itself must be of the substance of quicksilver—that is, metallic. For otherwise, it would have no way to enter into the metals. Furthermore, from where does this specific or "second" matter of Metals arise, if not from the "first" matter—namely, an **aqueous vapor** (a *watery steam* <note>original German: "einem Wasserschwaden"</note>)? This vapor, united with the Universal Salt of the world and nourished by the stars, finally receives from the elements its visible form.

But you say, "I do not understand the mixing and fixation of your **Niter** (that most abundant subject of the universal matter) with metals." You do not understand? Therefore, is it impossible? Do you believe that the Spirit of the World (which I know you will never go so far as to deny) in the belly of the ear—

...to adapt a body for itself and receive the faculties of all things, whether animal, vegetable, or mineral, just like a piece of wax capable of taking any form? Nor should it be difficult to believe that this corporeal spirit of the world <note>original: "spiritum mundi"</note>—namely, the **universal salt**—mixes in a certain way with the metals produced by it. Through this, their "accidents" <note>In alchemical philosophy, "accidents" are the outward physical traits like color or hardness, as opposed to the inner "essence."</note> (such as solidity, opacity, color, and the melting point they possess from their form) can be changed by that same spirit into thinness, semi-transparency, intense heat, and a fluidity like that of wax. 

But what prevents us from proceeding even further? Since you have the Mercury of Antimony <term>Antimony: A brittle, silvery-gray metalloid; in alchemy, it was often considered a "primary" material for transformation.</term>, in which the resolutive salt—or the **radical moisture** <note>The "radical moisture" was believed to be the fundamental life-fluid of all matter.</note> of metals—exists in an excellent degree, would it not be easy to extract a certain running Mercury <note>Quicksilver</note> from antimony or other minerals and metals without the help of "arousing salts"? I know you will admit here that you cannot. It follows, therefore, that the nature of these salts is **Mercurial**, as they act upon what is similar to themselves. 

What of the fact that a certain analogy and sympathy exists between the "sulfurs" of salts and metals? For it is certain that **Niter** <note>Saltpeter (potassium nitrate), vital for many alchemical and chemical processes.</note>, prepared in its own specific way, can induce a clearly golden color upon the **Regulus of Antimony** <term>Regulus: The metallic heart or "little king" of a mineral after the impurities (scoria) are removed.</term>, even if that color is not yet "fixed" <note>In alchemy, "fixed" means a permanent transformation that can withstand the fire of a furnace.</note>. For this I praise as a witness to the experiment my friend **Master Krafft** <note>Johann Joachim Krafft (1624–1697), a famous German chemist and alchemist who famously demonstrated phosphorus.</note>, an exceptional man currently spending time in *Dresden*. 

Indeed, I would say not only that salts cause metals to turn into mercury, but that these same salts—by the benefit of a most subtle "mercurial essence" of salts hidden in the air itself—are themselves changed into running Mercury. Nay, what is more, when joined with metals, they are transformed together into Mercury; and there is hope and confidence that this Mercury will not withdraw from them in terms of either form or matter. 

I. For it was mentioned by me above in Chapter V: **Eglinus** <note>Raphael Eglinus (1559–1622), a Swiss theologian and alchemist.</note>, using a peculiar instrument, drew **living silver** <note>Quicksilver/Mercury</note> out of the very air. 

II. I myself saw what **Antonius Hermannus**, the Master of Metals at *Eibenstock*, showed me: running Mercury collected by him that had fallen in the fog around the middle of May, 1672. 

III. I know the way in which, without distillation or sublimation, a true living Mercury ought to be prepared from **vitriol** <term>Vitriol: A sulfate of a metal, often green (iron) or blue (copper), used as a powerful corrosive.</term> through the air—or rather, through its spirit, the **Salt of the World**. 

IV. Nor am I lacking a process that teaches me how to produce such Mercury from **common salt**, or even from **tartar** <note>A byproduct of winemaking (potassium bitartrate).</note>, by a not very different method. 

V. That most curious scholar **Becher** <note>Johann Joachim Becher (1635–1682), a major figure in the history of chemistry and the developer of the phlogiston theory.</note> testifies in the first supplement of his *Subterranean Physics* that he saw metals made "attractive" and, when exposed to the air at night, turned into Mercury. 

VI. A few years ago, the Reverend **David Rebentrost**, the beloved Pastor of *Drebach*, produced the same from **Saturn** <note>The alchemical name for Lead.</note> with the help of **May Dew** <term>May Dew: Dew collected in early spring, believed by alchemists to be saturated with the "universal spirit" from the heavens.</term>.

<sig>Y 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: And</meta>

<page-num>166</page-num>
<header>EPILOGUE</header>
<meta>catchword: PHOS- (Note: Likely referring to "Phosphorus" or a similar light-bearing term on the following page.)</meta>

And Mercury **VII. Lord Pōlerus**, <note>Likely a contemporary alchemist or scholar known to the author.</note> famous for his chemical science, himself also found in **Dresden**, in silver mixed with several salts and exposed to the air for a long time. Indeed, **VIII.** all the "mercuries of bodies" <note>In alchemy, this refers to the volatile essence or "spirit" extracted from physical substances.</note> evaporating from here leave a red stain on the silver, attesting to the immortal soul of **Niter** <note>original: "Nitri"; Saltpeter.</note>.

From these things, since I trust it is clear enough, that **Spirit of the Universe**—both on its own in the macrocosm <note>original: "Universali majori"; the greater world or universe.</note> and through the mercuries of bodies produced by it—completes its entire work in the microcosm <note>original: "Universali minori"; the "lesser world," often referring to man or the laboratory vessel.</note>. Since this spirit of the world occurs most abundantly "embodied" in the Niter of the earth, we shall undoubtedly possess the **Salt of the World** <note>The author identifies this universal salt as the true "subject" or starting matter for the Great Work.</note> as the subject of the **Philosophers' Stone**—that is, the salt of the whole world. This excludes the "Mercury of Bodies," which we nonetheless establish as the subject of **Tinctures** <note>Tinctures were medicinal or transformative preparations, often considered a secondary or less powerful goal than the Stone itself.</note>, though with a great difference in power; it is not the time or situation to report on these things in more detail now.

Happy, therefore, three times and more, is the one who happens to thoroughly purge our Niter from all impurities and foreign salts! Such a person possesses a vessel of a great miracle—nay, a home for the Spirit, in which all volatile things find their rest. For here lies hidden the true fire of the Philosophers—not a bodily or material fire, but a spiritual or formal one. From this, that stone of the Philosophers (namely, a concentrated and fixed **starry fire** <note>original: "ignis astricus"</note>) is born. But let us take our hand from the tablet <note>An idiom meaning "to stop working" or "to end the discussion."</note>. Let us therefore strive in this **Great Work** <term>Great Work: (Latin: Magnum Opus) The process of creating the Philosophers' Stone.</term> and through effective observations rather than speculation and mere reasoning, since it would be ridiculous to invent something and thrust it upon the world as a matter of belief if you have no personal experience of it yourself. Meanwhile, sealing this entire matter with the precept of **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy.</note>, our Father, from the *Book of Roots*: **The wisdom of the Author should be greater than his book**. Having promised me your favor, I bid you to be as well as can be, whoever you are, most kind Reader.

<summary>The author concludes that the "Spirit of the World" is embodied in purified Niter, which serves as the true foundation for the Philosophers' Stone. He ends with a call for practical experimentation over theoretical speculation, citing the authority of Hermes Trismegistus.</summary>

<keywords>Niter, Philosophers' Stone, Lord Polerus, Spirit of the Universe, Starry Fire, Great Work, Hermes Trismegistus, Tinctures, Dresden</keywords>

# THE HERMETIC
## *Phosphorus,* <note>original: "Phosphorus Hermeticus"; literally "The Hermetic Light-Bearer." This refers to Balduin's discovery of a phosphorescent substance, likely a form of calcium nitrate, which emitted light in the dark after being exposed to the sun.</note>

# THE LUMINOUS
### *M A G N E T* <note>original: "Magnes Luminaris"; Balduin called his phosphorus a "magnet" because he believed it "attracted" the light of the sun or the "spirit of the world" from the air.</note>

## BY CHRISTIAN ADOLPH
## BALDUIN,

->Fellow of the Holy Roman Imperial Academy of the Curious in Nature,<-
->surnamed Hermes.<- <note>Members of this prestigious scientific society, now known as the Leopoldina, were traditionally given classical or mythic "cognomina" or surnames; Balduin was appropriately named after Hermes, the patron of alchemy.</note>

### Madrigal. <note>A short, lyrical poem popular in the 17th century, often used as a dedication or introductory praise.</note>

> **T**he New World would
> Still be hidden behind its sea,
> Had not a clever hero,
> *Flavius* of *Amalfi*, <note>original: "Flavius von Cosen"; likely referring to Flavio Gioja of Amalfi, the legendary (though perhaps mythical) Italian inventor of the mariner's compass.</note>

> Yet only one thing is still lacking,
> By which the difficult voyages

<meta>This page continues the introductory front matter for Balduin's treatise on his "Hermetic Phosphorus." <page-num>168</page-num>
<header>HERMETIC PHOSPHORUS.</header>
<meta>catchword: chap.</meta>

->Your self-invented Phosphorus,<-
->Which with transfigured radiance<-
->Must steer the ship of Jason.<- <note>The *Argo*, Jason's ship. In alchemical literature, the quest for the Golden Fleece was often used as a metaphor for the search for the Philosophers' Stone.</note>
->Here shines a new star: we sail bright and fast<-
->And see it from afar: there hangs the Golden Fleece!<-

->This was written out of compelling duty /<-
->as an everlasting remembrance / to his most<-
->worthy friend<-
->Johann Engelhart,<-
->Candidate of Medicine.<-

---

->C. D.<- <note>Likely an abbreviation for *Cum Deo*, Latin for "With God."</note>

Nothing is more beautiful than light, nothing more sweet, nothing more wondrous. If it were not for this, the world itself would not be beautiful. Hence, light and the universe share the same birthday. Light was the first adornment of the world, its first perfection.

There is, moreover, a certain sparkling—if not in all, then certainly in very many natural bodies—and it flows out from them, as it were: as in the *scolopendra*, <note>original: "Assel"; here referring to a centipede or woodlouse</note> that insect which shines in the dark, see *Athanasius Kircher, book 1, part 5, The Great Art of Light and Shadow, chapter 6, section 1.* It is also found in the lion, horses, bulls, wolves, lambs, dogs, foxes, the cat, the hyena, birds, fish, mice, snakes, etc., according to *Thomas Bartholin, book 2, chapter 2 and following.* Among these, the *lampyrides* <note>Fireflies</note> are especially conspicuous, as noted by *Petrus Johannes Faber in the Spagyric Palladium, chapter 17, page 165,* and the *cicindelæ* <note>Glow-worms</note> by *Kircher in On Light and Shadow, book 1, chapter 6.* It is found in plants, such as the *starry fungus* and the *baara* <note>A legendary glowing herb described by the historian Josephus</note>, as well as in decaying wood. It is even found in the meat of a wether <note>A castrated ram</note>, which shone at night like many fireflies, as reported by *Pierre Borel in his Medico-Physical History and Observations, Century 1, Observation 3.* 

It was seen in a noble girl of most graceful form, who, while loosening her hair tangled in a comb, shook out flames like burning stars from her head into her lap, as recorded by *Juan Eusebio Nieremberg, book 1, chapter 7 of his Natural History of Foreign Objects.* It was seen in a woman's petticoat, which sparkled as witnessed by *Francis Bacon in his Natural and Universal Philosophy.* It is found in sea water, which casts out much light, according to *Nicolas Papin in his French treatise, On the Light of the Sea, and Balthasar de Monconys in volume 1 of his Travels.* Finally, it is found in gems and stones, for example in the *carbuncle*, <note>A term used historically for any red gemstone, like a garnet or ruby, believed to emit its own light like a glowing coal</note> which holds the sovereignty among all burning gems, as stated by *Isidore of Seville, book 16.*

<summary>This page serves as a dedication and a philosophical introduction to light, cataloging numerous historical accounts of bioluminescence in animals, humans, and minerals to provide a natural context for Balduin's "Hermetic Phosphorus."</summary>
<keywords>phosphorus, bioluminescence, Jason and the Argonauts, Golden Fleece, Athanasius Kircher, Francis Bacon, carbuncle, light, natural philosophy</keywords>

This serves as the scientific and alchemical justification for the author's discovery of "Hermetic Phosphorus."</meta>

# OR THE LUMINOUS MAGNET.
Y
<meta>catchword: see-</meta>

---

<margin>chapter 13. Pliny, book 2, chapter 8. Anselm Boëtius de Boodt, History of Gems, book 2, chapter 8.</margin> Although there are very learned men who (quite plausibly) deny that any carbuncles <note>original: "carbunculos"; historically, "carbuncle" referred to red gemstones, like rubies or garnets, that were legendary for their ability to glow in the dark.</note> or glowing gems exist: <margin>Boëtius de Boodt, History of Gems and Stones, book 2, chapter 8. Joannes de Laet in his chapter on Carbuncles and Rubies. Museum Wormianum, chapter 17, page 103.</margin> But read *Benvenuto Cellini, On the Art of Jewelry, book 1, page 10.* On the diamond, see <margin>Mr. Clayton, Mr. Robert Boyle, Noble Englishman, Narration of certain observations on the said stone, page 433.</margin> and on a similar diamond, which by the help of water just a little more than lukewarm he brought to such a state that it shone in the dark, see <margin>the said Author in his Specimen on the Origin and Virtues of Gems, page 124.</margin> On the *Bologna Stone*, <note>original: "Lapide Bononiensi"; a type of barium sulfate found near Bologna which, when calcined, becomes phosphorescent. It was a sensation in 17th-century science.</note> which, prepared by a certain method and exposed to light, so drinks it in, so tenaciously incorporates it into itself, that when it is taken from its box and placed in a dark spot, it spreads the light it has conceived and preserved, not unlike living coals, to the wonder of those watching. See <margin>Mr. Ole Worm in the Museum, chapter 5, page 46, and Mr. Fortunio Liceti, who wrote on this expressly. Also Mazetta P. Borm. in Philosophy.</margin> Its mineral is found near *Toulouse*, where they are accustomed to dig for alum stones, commonly called *Rock Alum*, as taught by <margin>Father Athanasius Kircher in the Magnetic Art, Book 3, part 4, question 2, pages 463, 464, and 469.</margin>

From this, *Mr. Jan Baptista van Helmont* <note>A famous Flemish chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern chemistry.</note> concludes in the treatise *The Great Opportunity, folio 97, number 35,* that Light is truly an entity existing outside of its source. For, he writes, I keep in my possession a flint which, if I expose it to the air while the Sun is above the horizon for the space of at least three or four pauses, and then carry it to a dark place, it preserves the light of the Sun it has conceived, perhaps for some similar duration.

While I was weighing these things with myself several times, I began at the same time both to think about the luminous stone, and whether the same thing could be prepared from our most Universal matter. Indeed, I allowed myself to be led by these reasons: because I knew that Philosophical fire <note>In alchemy, "Philosophical fire" often refers to a secret, non-burning heat or a concentrated spiritual energy within matter.</note> was present within it; nor did I remember any who declared it impossible; and—what is the "head and foot" of the matter, as they say—I had read what *Mr. D. B.* (whose manuscript I keep in my possession) wrote about N.: that when gold <note>original: "☉", the symbol for Sol/Gold.</note> was dissolved by himself in a Philosophical Solvent, <note>original: "Menstruô Phil."; "Menstruum" was the alchemical term for a solvent.</note> the solvent changed nothing of its color; but at night, it provided the appearance of a most burning or brilliant light, so that by its glow, not only did everything seem clearly distinguished, but even the smallest characters could be read and written, just as if someone had lit candles arranged here and there.

And so, only a very few days ago, when I had placed my zeal and labor into making the *Alkahest*, <note>The "Alkahest" was the hypothetical "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, capable of dissolving any substance into its primary matter.</note> once the distillation was completed, I discovered that the glass retort, <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation.</note> once cooled, was glowing inside like red-hot iron, and had been turned into a Luminous Stone. It was believable that it had performed what...

<meta>This page continues the author's description of his "Hermetic Phosphorus." The previous page ended mid-sentence discussing how the Bologna Stone spreads light "not unlike" another substance. This page picks up with the author's own observations of his "Alkahest" (a universal solvent) and his naming of the "Luminous Magnet."</meta>

### HERMETIC PHOSPHORUS

...I had seen my *Alkahest* <note>original: "Alkahest"; in alchemy, this refers to a mythical universal solvent capable of dissolving any substance into its primary components.</note> which would have penetrated it. Truly, what a wonder of Nature! Therefore, no name occurred to me as more suitable for this offspring—at least for the present—than that which I have written on the title page: **HERMETIC PHOSPHORUS**, or the **LUMINOUS MAGNET**. For from that, I seem to have observed the following:

(1.) That this Phosphorus, during the day, draws in such splendor from the light of the Sun (it matters little whether the day be cloudy or clear) within a few minutes of a single hour, that it appears clearly fiery and sparkling, to the great admiration of everyone. From this, one may conclude that the Moon likewise contains the splendor of the Sun in the same way. Thus, it may come to mind that the ancient Philosophers called their universal subject—from which this Phosphorus is made—by the name *Lunaria* <note>original: "Lunariam"; literally "Moon-plant," a term used by alchemists to describe the material used to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> for that very reason. This is that celestial fire which *Prometheus*, the son of *Iapetus*, secretly stole from the heavens on the advice of *Minerva*. It is that invisible fire of *Zoroaster* and *Heraclitus*, of which *Chalcidius* speaks. Hence comes the fiery Spirit of *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>original: "Mercurii Trismegisti"</note> and other Ancients.

(2.) How small is the light of a lit candle? And yet, the fire hidden within this Phosphorus of mine attracts that light, or those fiery atoms, and is made to shine itself, even if it is not moved closer to the candle. By this experiment, the doctrine of atoms may be established.

(3.) Since I was not ignorant of the fact that a magnetic needle inside glass (even a vacuum) is moved by a common magnet applied to the outside, I myself, eager for knowledge, enclosed my Luminous Magnet in glass. Nevertheless, it attracted light from the sun and from a lit candle, just as it ought to do. From this, it is clearer than the midday Sun that glass has pores; for not only can the most subtle air enter and exit secretly, but material light is also able to penetrate it. *According to Father Gaspar Schott, in "Curious Arts," book 4, chapter 5, search 1, paragraph 2; see also Observation 62 by Mr. Olaus Borrichius in the "Medical and Physical Acts" of Mr. Bartholin, years 1671 and 1672.*

(4.) When placed against a white wall, my Phosphorus does not merely shine in the dark; it also illustrates and scatters light, following the example of the Moon, which itself bestows borrowed light from the Sun upon the world.

(5.) Indeed, when put away in a hidden place for some time, the Phosphorus lays aside its acquired light as if putting it down. It is likely that this light migrates back to the Sun—that *Fiery Sea* <note>original: "Mare igneum"; a cosmological concept where the sun acts as a vast reservoir of all the world's fire and light.</note> (as *Mr. Sebastian Birdig* calls it in "The New Medicine of the Spirit," book 1, chapter 27, paragraph 4)—or perhaps into the air in the meantime. However, when returned to public light, or at least to a lit candle, it takes up again what it previously laid down...

<meta>catchword: suum (his/its own)</meta>

<summary>The author details five observations regarding his "Hermetic Phosphorus," arguing that it absorbs and emits light like a "Luminous Magnet" and serves as physical proof of the existence of atoms and the porosity of glass.</summary>

<page-num>171</page-num>
<header>OR THE LUMINOUS MAGNET</header>
<meta>catchword: is</meta>
<sig>Z 2</sig>

its own splendor. Indeed, this substance will provide this for you as often as you like, and for all time.

(6.) When it had shown itself to be at its most brilliant, I placed my Phosphorus into a glass full of the <term>Philosophical Spirit</term> <note>represented by the symbol $\Phi$, likely referring to the "spirit of the philosophers" or the "Alkahest" mentioned previously.</note>. However, far from losing any of its splendor, it instead persisted just as it had before.

(7.) I then took it out and dried it in a hidden or dark place so that it might lose the light it had produced. Once this was done, I enclosed it again in the same way I mentioned before, in a glass filled with cold water, to be more certain about the light; for I believed it would again attract light from the clear air. My hope did not fail me. For immediately it took on a fiery glow, as bright as it ever gets, so that it clearly appears from this, I think, that neither the <term>philosophical fire</term> <note>A term for the internal energy or "soul" of a substance in alchemy.</note> enclosed within it, nor the attracted splendor, is so weak that the power of the cold could extinguish or hinder it.

(8.) But then once more, my Phosphorus, returning to its previous state after this was done, laid aside the light of which we have spoken so much. Therefore, I placed it on a hot furnace, wishing to test whether the light would return. And indeed it did return, and it began to radiate as before, even though the room where I had placed it was devoid of light.

(9.) From this came the opinion that this **Luminous Magnet** does not only enjoy a light borrowed from elsewhere, but shines with its own internal fire. Yet it does not do so unless you first excite it with some external force. It is much like the way white-hot iron shines with an innate light, but one that has been ignited by fire. In the same way, the illumination of the Moon tends to happen not by some reflection, but by a kind of "kindling."

(10.) Therefore, to give a true account of this light, I suppose that the air, illuminated by the Sun or by fire, is filled with a very subtle vapor that is most fit for receiving light. For <term>lux</term> <note>original: "lux"; in the Latin of this period, "lux" refers to light at its source (like the sun or a flame).</note> remains in the light-source, while <term>lumen</term> <note>original: "lumen"; refers to the radiance or glow that travels through a medium like air.</note> proceeds from the light. *Lux* is in the Sun; *lumen* is in the air. The Sun is light-giving; the air is illuminated. Or, according to Paradox 7 of Master <term>Wirdig</term> <note>Sebastian Wirdig (1613–1687), a physician and professor who wrote on the "medicine of spirits" and magnetic sympathies.</note>, Book 1, Chapter 7: Fire, *lux*, and *lumen* are spirits; therefore:

(11.) Because in my Phosphorus, the philosophical fire undoubtedly fulfills and takes on the role of the "center" (which is the nature of a magnet), it cannot fail to happen that, because of a mutual sympathy, it seizes and unexpectedly takes up that fire or splendor which the radiating Sun and fire likewise cast out, breathe, and scatter.

(12.) Just as many wonders can be shown by the benefit of this Hermetic Phosphorus (which anyone can think of, according to the...)

<summary>The author describes experiments where his phosphorus retains its glow in philosophical spirits and cold water, and regains it when heated. He theorizes that the substance acts as a "magnet" for light, possessing an internal fire that must be "kindled" by external sources.</summary>

<page-num>172</page-num>
<header>HERMETIC PHOSPHORUS, OR THE MAGNET OF LIGHT.</header>

is [achieved] by the sagacity of the intellect, it becomes whole; I hold it thus, and I am entirely of this mind: that this especially must be tried:

(13.) Whether from the same principle—namely the <term>Alkahest</term> <note>A hypothetical "universal solvent" in alchemy, capable of reducing any body to its primary matter.</note> (which I would rightly call fire reduced into a center, I think)—the perpetual fire of the ancients might be conveniently restored. The famous scholar *Kircher* <note>Athanasius Kircher (1602–1680), a Jesuit polymath who expressed skepticism about "eternal lamps" in his work *Mundus Subterraneus*.</note> did not allow himself to doubt this, as we read in his reports in *Subterranean World, Book 8, Section 3, page 71*. That such a fire once existed in the nature of things is testified to by many passages from the most esteemed authors. See, among others: *Petrus Apianus, Bartholomaeus Amantius, Coelius Rhodiginus, Lord Fortunio Liceti: On the Lamps of the Ancients, Book 1, Chapter 1; Lord Ernst Burggrav in the Biolychnium; Lord Daniel Sennert in the chapter on long fasting; Alessandro Tassoni in his Inquiries, Book 1, Question 26; Hermolaus Barbarus, Book 5 in the Corollary; Marcus Zuerius Boxhorn in Roman Inquiries, page 49; Pancirolli, Memorable Things Lost, Part 1, Title 35, page 124.*

(14.) Furthermore, we are most firmly convinced that the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>The legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting immortality.</note> (which we hope will emerge from the most universal matter) must necessarily and indefinitely emit light. This is because it is nothing other than fire enclosed in a center. Although in all the books of the Philosophers that I have happened to see, there is not a trace or footprint of this property, I recently read a handwritten account by *Fridericus Gallus* <note>Possibly a Latinization of "Frederick the Frenchman" or a specific alchemical pseudonym.</note> concerning his journey. He says he met a man whom we call an Anchorite <note>A religious recluse or hermit.</note>, as his religious devotion had led him away from the rest of the living. He was called *Father Michael*, and he was born from the noble *Trautmansdorff* family. His monastery was located four miles from *Landsberg*. Here, Gallus saw a truly wonderful thing! He says the hermit secretly showed him a <term>Tincture</term> <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" often refers to the Philosopher's Stone in its potent, liquid, or concentrated form.</note> which, in terms of size, did not much exceed a large bean. Its color was like that of a garnet stone, of which many are brought from Bohemia. Its chief quality was its splendor. You would have believed it to be a lamp burning inside a glass.

This is certainly a matter most worthy of our study and effort to investigate and explain. <note>original Greek: Χαλεπὰ τὰ καλὰ</note> "Fine things are difficult." Let my most honored colleagues exercise the powers of their intellect with me in this admirable work and overcome the difficulty of the task with relentless labor. They shall carry back the greatest utility joined with equal glory from the completed work.

He supports this with a contemporary account of a glowing "Tincture" possessed by a hermit of the Trautmansdorff family.</summary>

# LETTER
### of a certain most learned man
#### containing
# A JUDGMENT
#### Regarding
# THE GOLD OF THE AIR
### recently published.
### THE YEAR 1674.

---

-> MOST EXCELLENT SIR; <-

By this opportune occasion, the bearer of this letter will return to your Excellency—in my name and with honorable thanks—the dissertation concerning **THE GOLD OF THE AIR** <note>original: "DE AURO AURÆ". This refers to a work by Christian Adolf Balduin (1632–1682), a German alchemist who discovered a type of luminous calcium nitrate he called "Hermetic Phosphorus."</note> by the Lord Inventor (to be explained anagrammatically), which you sent to me to examine not so many days ago. And since it is already circulating in the hands of many curious and otherwise most noble men of truly significant erudition—and as one person brings this judgment and another brings that, according to the diversity of their grasp <note>original: "pro captus diversitate"</note> on the matter—your Excellency, to whom I am always otherwise bound, thought it not for nothing to also desire my own mind's judgment. 

I could not, nor should I, fail your most honorable desire, relying on this most certain confidence: that you will receive this writing of my meager talent with a fair and benevolent mind, valuing the will for the deed. How great is the curiosity of mortals, and how much those people—by adding new inventions to inventions and commending themselves to posterity—pant for a fame of name that will not die out; this present example, regarding which your Excellency provided me the opportunity to discourse, proves this among other things.

<sig>z 3</sig>

<meta>This page continues the "Judgment" or critique of Christian Adolf Balduin's work on the "Gold of the Air." # JUDGMENT ON THE GOLD OF THE AIR

Truly, the **AUTHOR** of this dissertation, whoever he may be—one recognizes the lion by its claw <note>original: "ex ungue leonem", a proverb meaning that a great thinker's quality can be judged by even a small sample of their work</note>—is a most learned man, well-read in the authors of both ancient and modern philosophers, and possesses a sincere and most sharp wit and judgment. His writing should not be denied praise for its skillfully and exquisitely delivered points. However, one or two small doubts <note>original: "scrupulum", literally a small sharp stone in one's shoe</note> present themselves to me, which require a clearer explanation.

The distinction already handed down by the ancients, which the **LORD AUTHOR OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR** repeats in chapter 4, pages 29, 30, and following—namely into **Astral, Elemental, and Metallic Gold**—does not seem to me to be so well-suited to the way Nature works. For in the production of natural things, the **SUN** <note>original: "SOL"</note> acts as a universal and indifferent efficient cause, not as a special and specific one. Specific identity is granted by the specific form of any natural composite, which is what the **SUN** is. For this reason, the resulting object does not later participate in the essence of the universal efficient cause; otherwise, we would have to claim that all things drawn from the triple kingdom of the world—vegetables, animals, and other minerals—are also "astral" and "elemental," etc. The **SUN** permeates, animates, and preserves all these things equally in their being, but only in a universal and indifferent way according to the nature and logic of each species.

How then can **GOLD** be the natural son of the **SUN**? Nor does the reason he provides—derived from a powder of dark or purple color verging on red, collected from solar rays by means of an artfully arranged glass vessel—seem to prove anything. This powder was perhaps collected not from the solar rays themselves, but from the **Salt of the Air** <note>original: "Sale aëris". In the 17th century, this often referred to aerial nitre or saltpeter, which alchemists believed was a physical manifestation of the sun's energy.</note> through the concentration and reverberation of the radiating solar heat (page 30). For various kinds of **Salts** circulate in the **Air** <note>original: "Aurâ"</note>; by the help of subterranean fires and the operation of the sun, these salts are turned into vapors <note>original: "meteorisata"</note> and seek the heavens, provided by the nature of the ground beneath—be they nitrous, vitriolic, aluminous, or of another nature, either separately or mixed together.

Thus it is well known that a not insignificant quantity of true and genuine vitriol, and likewise of impure Nitre, was collected at various times of the year from the air of Amsterdam—which is impregnated with the miasmas of many salt waters and marshy soil—by means of a certain ingenious glass. This is attested by the *Famous Morhof* <note>Daniel Georg Morhof (1639–1691), a prominent German scholar and historian of alchemy.</note> in his *Letter on the Transmutation of Metals*. What is more, the diversity of the seasons supplies different kinds of salts in the air; summer produces "alkalized" salts of the air, while winter produces "acidic vitriolic" salts, either separately or mixed, as the change in the air brings them. However, I would not conclude that these salts affect a state of "universality," or "panurgia," or "panspermia" <note>The theory that "seeds" of all things exist everywhere in the air.</note>. Indeed, salts of an architectural nature, as...

<summary>A critical response to the theory of "Gold of the Air," arguing that the "astral gold" supposedly collected from sunlight is actually just common atmospheric salts like nitre or vitriol concentrated by heat.</summary>

# RECENTLY PUBLISHED

...and that fire acts as an instrument, we easily grant, but not of a single kind; rather, it varies just as bodies are various and the mixtures of bodies are diverse. By the help of these, and according to the nature of the subjects, Nature employs fire in their composition and resolution <note>resolution: the chemical process of breaking a substance down into its component parts</note>. Nature also perfects wonderful operations in the bodies of the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms with the aid of **salts**, which disperse subterranean **fire-treasuries** <note>original: "pyrophylacia," referring to hypothesized internal chambers of fire or heat within the earth</note> through all the passages of the earth, and all the veins and mines of metals. 

How then do these things—as external instruments belonging to the efficient cause—flow into the **BEING** of one thing caused proximately by another? Or who would say they pertain to the constitution of its substance? The **LORD INVENTOR OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR** says on pages 43, 44, etc., that all things can be resolved into salt and also consist of it. 

"We know by chemical doctrine," he says, "that salt is produced in all parts of the world by the natural heat of the world itself. While this heat acts upon its own moisture by consuming it, so that it may be nourished by it, it dries that moisture. That which has been dried within the natural moisture itself is **natural Salt**, and it is the natural [essence] of any thing, since there is nothing that cannot be resolved into it." 

From these words, it is clear that the aforementioned **LORD AUTHOR** establishes that there is no thing entirely devoid of Salt; indeed, he claims the constitution of natural things primarily depends on Salt and is resolved back into it. 

Truly, this is a great assumption <note>original: "petitum," a premise claimed without proof</note>. There are many natural composites devoid of Salt, from which not even a scrap <note>original: "ne gry quidem," a Greek idiom for the smallest possible amount</note> of Salt can be elicited—such as from lettuce, endive, gourd, etc. And who can extract it from the **Sun** <note>Gold</note>, the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>, talc, pearls, etc.? Intense fire reduces these not to lime, but to a molten state. 

For as far as the **SALT OF GOLD** is concerned, the Noble Englishman **Robert Boyle** says in *The Sceptical Chymist* <note>original: "chemiſtâ Sceptico," page 100</note>: "It was never granted to me to behold it, nor has it been proved by the testimony of any trustworthy eyewitness, that such a thing was ever separated in nature." 

If, however, the extraction of some "solar salt" in chemical processes must be performed according to a prescription using corrosive **solvents** <term>menstrua: alchemical liquids used to dissolve solids</term> or the intervention of other saline bodies, a doubt will remain for a curious and cautious man: does that **SALT** emerge from the **GOLD** itself, or from the saline bodies or spirits used to prepare it? 

Whether **SALTS** are actually present within the natural composite things from which they are drawn, or whether they are created out of them [by the process], we shall see. The late **Dr. Sennert** <note>Daniel Sennert (1572–1637), a famous physician who sought to reconcile Aristotelianism with alchemy and atomism</note> defended the former opinion along with the Chemists; they establish that **SALTS** are truly present in things formally, and are only separated by chemical art. The **LORD INVENTOR OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR** also seems to be of this number. 

In truth, salts—and especially **alkalis**—neither exist before the composition of bodies, nor do they flow together to create the mixture, nor finally, through natural...

# A JUDGMENT ON THE GOLD OF THE AIR
<meta>catchword: fi</meta>

...ever appeared in nature through the breaking down of bodies, but instead are only brought out by the art of fire and proceed from it effectively. Thus, these alkaline salts existed in the composite body before it was burned only materially and in "passive potential"—as the **Scholastic teachers** <note>The Scholastics were medieval scholars who used Aristotelian logic to explain philosophy and theology.</note> say—but not formally in the shape of an alkali. On this point, the famous **Van Helmont** <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering chemist who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern science.</note> speaks most subtly and sharply in his treatise *The Human Blast*: "Alkali," he says, "is not generated in the ash by fire as an efficient cause, even though it proceeds from it effectively. For the duty of fire is indeed to ignite, to consume, and to separate, but not to produce anything. Rather, while fixed alkali is made from salt that was previously volatile, this is not the production of a new thing, but merely the alteration of a thing." 

Indeed, the alkali was present materially in the composite before cremation. However, while the fire removes the **mercury** and **sulfur**, the **Salt**—as a principle that survives better in the melting of the fire—seizes a neighboring part of the sulfur (or fatness). Because the salt cannot fully protect that sulfur from the "torture of the fire," part of it flies away under the mask of <term>Gas</term> <note>Van Helmont coined the word "Gas," deriving it from the Greek word "Chaos," to describe substances that were neither solid nor liquid.</note> and acquires the smell of burning, while another part is incorporated so that it becomes true charcoal. Therefore, the Sulfur, now fixed by its marriage to the Salt, does not quickly turn from charcoal into soot, but does so gradually and only in an open vessel. Finally, it flies away with its previous sulfur (for this is why sulfur mostly retains the sharp taste of volatile Salt) and at last with the "charcoal-sulfur," carrying the proper weight of its own volatile salt with it. And in Section 43 [of Helmont's work], he writes: "I have learned, namely, that every combustible body has within itself a **Volatile Salt**, which, by the seizing of its own sulfur, is fixed into an **Alkali**." And Section 41 fits here: "Decayed and rotten wood leaves behind almost no Salt in its ashes. This is because the volatile Salt departed with the sulfur through the ferment of rot." 

**Tachenius** <note>Otto Tachenius (1610–1699), a German chemist and pharmacist who popularized Van Helmont's ideas.</note> repeats these same things from the words of **Van Helmont** 
<margin>Helmont 10</margin>
in his *Hippocrates Chimicus*, chapter 21: "No vegetable has a **FIXED SALT**, of whatever kind it may be, unless it is burned by actual fire, so that by scorching, the acid can act upon and seize the alkali, and vice versa." 

Truly, although this <note>original: "κεινόμενον," likely a misspelling of "κινόμενον," referring to the proposition or point in motion</note> **proposition**—namely, that no vegetable actually contains alkali within itself—is not far from the truth, "storms of doubt" nevertheless arise as to whether it is produced on this worldly stage by the **art of Vulcan** <note>Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; here, it refers to the alchemist's use of the furnace.</note> in the way Helmont and Tachenius explain. For indeed, no **SALT**, whether volatile or fixed alkali, exists actually and formally in the vegetable compounds; rather, it exists in some (though not in all, as the Author claims) only in potentiality and materially, still immersed in other congenital sulfurous and spirituous principles. Now...

# RECENTLY PUBLISHED
<note>The header refers to a recent publication being critiqued by the author. A faint ghosting of text from the reverse page is visible at the very top of the page above the header.</note>

...if fire exercises its violence upon a subject capable of it, it immediately attacks the symbolic oily and sulfurous parts. In these parts, <term>volatile salt</term> <note>original: *sal volatile*; a salt that easily evaporates, often associated with the "spirit" of a substance.</note> lies hidden materially and potentially. This salt, wishing to seek safety in flight, partly departs into <term>soot</term> <note>original: *fuliginem*</note> and is partly seized by <term>acid spirit</term>—not without some earthy parts adhering to it—in a pleasing union, where it takes on the nature of an <term>alkali</term> <note>A fixed salt that remains after combustion.</note>.

The foundation of this assertion is sought from nature itself and from chemical mechanics. Even if you reduce <term>rotten wood</term> <note>original: *Ligna putrida*</note> to ashes with the greatest possible care, you will elicit no salt from it. The reason is that rotten wood has already been resolved by the salt existing in the air through internal corruption, without the need for violent combustion. This happened before any violent resolution by fire was instituted to separate its principles. And so, before the **Salt**—which exists potentially in vegetables—could be reduced into action, it vanished along with the other sulfurous and native spirituous principles to which it was still married. Thus, by natural law and necessity, it was volatilized along with those principles and passed into <term>Gas</term> <note>A term coined by Van Helmont to describe the invisible "spirit" or "chaos" released from matter.</note>.

In vegetables, distillation through a <term>retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids.</note> always reveals an acid spirit, accompanied by <term>empyreumatic</term> <note>Burnt-smelling.</note> oils swollen with volatile salt; this is evident from the distillation of <term>tartar</term> <note>The crust formed in wine casks.</note>, guaiacum wood, boxwood, and others. We know very well from chemical practice how much volatile salt and thick, stinking oil the distillation of soot provides, joined with a very great abundance of acid <term>phlegm</term> <note>The watery, tasteless portion of a distillate.</note>. Finally, concerning the **volatile salts** produced not only by the animal kingdom but also the vegetable kingdom (such as tartar, wine dregs, and distilled vegetable oils), these are not formally present in their composite bodies. Rather, they are so materially immersed that they are only elicited at the end of a distillation performed by a retort exposed to an open fire, once the texture of the composite and the shape of the particles have been destroyed by the supreme and violent torture of the flame. No taste of this is perceivable in the natural composite before distillation.

As for the **MAGNETISM** by which he binds that **GOLD OF THE AIR**—specifically the **ASTRAL GOLD**—as if with shackles, he states on pages 20, 22, as well as 46, 47, and 51, that it is <term>nitre</term> <note>Saltpeter, often believed by alchemists to contain a "celestial balsam" or "universal salt."</note>. He proposes that it should be prepared in the following way:

**TAKE** of the whitest and purest nitre as much as you wish. Let there be four baked bricks, between which the crucible containing the nitre is placed. With fire applied from below for two hours, under a clear sky, when the Sun is entering either **Cancer**, **Leo**, or **Virgo** in the morning time, they are restrained. Once this is done, that nitre (or "magnetism") is seen to revive in a short time, to move, and within the space of about a hundred minutes to attract the **Golden Sulfur**, called the **GOLD OF THE AIR**, into...

### JUDGMENT ON THE GOLD OF THE AIR

According to the **INVENTOR**, you will see that from the **AIR** <note>original: *ex AURA*; refers to the alchemical concept of the vital or astral atmosphere.</note>, through **magnetism**, he has attracted a grass-green color both inside and out, and the highest degree of it; however, after it has been cooled, it assumes a blue color, such as that usually associated with Uranus <note>original: *Uranii*; likely referring to the sky-blue color or the "heavenly" quality rather than the planet discovered later.</note>. In the elaboration of this, these precautions must be observed: if you apply a slightly stronger fire to the **green lion** <note>original: *viridi leoni*; a common alchemical term for raw, mineral matter or vitriol capable of transformation.</note> within the magnetism, it will return to its own chaos and leave the magnetism or **nitre** <note>original: *nitrum*; saltpeter or a similar salt used as a "magnet" for spirits.</note> completely empty and become white as it was before. Therefore, while it is still warm and imbued with the **green lion**, pour the magnetism into a mortar and grind it quickly.

If you dissolve this in spring water, it will turn green and precipitate into a purple powder, which must be washed. In the same way, if you pour **Spirit of Wine** <note>original: *Spiritum vini*; concentrated alcohol.</note> over this magnetism once it has been reduced to powder and allow these things to digest, it will gently extract the "blood of the green lion" or the **Golden Sulphur** in a red form, for preparing the **POTABLE GOLD OF THE AIR**. If you wish to prepare **Potable Gold** <note>original: *AURUM potabile*; a legendary alchemical medicine consisting of gold in liquid form.</note> from this, take this precipitated Golden Sulphur, wash it, and fix it by joining it with the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *mundi Spiritu*; the universal vital essence believed to permeate the air.</note> according to the rules of the art. Or, if you are unskilled in these things, distill the Spirit of Wine tinged with the **Gold of the Air** until a fourth part of it remains; you will have a residue of Potable Gold, most pleasing to the sight in its redness. Although this may initially show some saltiness, after a few days the **Magnetism** settles like clear water with the tincture floating on top. You should protect this in glass from the sun's rays, for if exposed to the sun, this Gold is soon bleached of its color. These are the claims of the **LORD INVENTOR CONCERNING THE GOLD OF THE AIR**.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Truly, I would easily yield to the genius of our **INVENTOR**, provided it were animated by experimental certainty. For his preparation of the magnetism leaves a doubt in my mind: whether that **Golden Sulphur** or **GOLD OF THE AIR** was really attracted from the **Air** by the magnetism (or fixed nitre), or rather from the flame of the fire which contains acid within itself. For whoever knows how the flame of fire can often produce remarkable alterations in the arrangement of the parts of bodies through various mutations of texture and figure—even if no Golden Sulphur or Gold of the Air has come from the Atmosphere—he will hold his judgment in suspense regarding this taught preparation of the Magnetism and will always fluctuate in uncertainty. 

Who, by this reasoning, would not also take the **Tincture of Salt of Tartar** <note>original: *Salis Tartari*; potassium carbonate.</note>—extracted with spirit of wine from Salt of Tartar calcined to a blue color by the benefit of fire—for a genuine **Solar Tincture** <note>A "Solar" tincture refers to one possessing the qualities of Gold.</note>? What is it that adorns **steel** <note>original: *chalybem*</note> heated in glowing coals with such various colors, so delightful to the sight? What grants such a great change of colors to **lead** <note>original: *plumbo*</note> exposed to the flames of **Vulcan** <note>A mythological reference to Fire.</note>? See the aforementioned **Robert Boyle** in his treatise *On Colors*. These things are of such great weight that anyone...

...the blood of the green lion. <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "it will gently extract the blood of the green lion."</note>

Having performed the attraction of **GOLD** from the **AIR** <note>original: *ex AURA*; referring to the vital or atmospheric spirit</note> by means of **magnetism**, one might not without merit come to this opinion and decide: that this **Magnetism**—or **nitre** <note>saltpeter</note>—having been deprived of its native moisture by the flame of fire, has been impregnated in turn by the most acidic part of the flame (though not without some earthy parts clinging to it). When this substance is later entrusted to moist air, in which the **Salt of the Air** is undoubtedly hidden, it undergoes various changes in its texture and its parts, both on the surface and internally. These changes are the causes of the different colors, just as in the examples of **salt of tartar**, **steel**, **lead**, etc., mentioned above.

From this it happens that when **spirit of wine** <note>concentrated alcohol</note> is poured over the nitre (which has been imbued with the **Eranean** tincture <note>original: *tincturâ Eraneâ*; likely referring to a sky-blue or violet-purple hue, similar to the "Uranian" color mentioned previously</note>), the "acid of the flame" concentrated within it—due to the alteration and change in the texture and shape of its parts—transforms into the inventor’s **Potable Gold** <note>original: *Aurum potabile*; a legendary medicinal elixir made of dissolved gold</note>. As for the powder that settles at the bottom, I recognize it merely as earthy dross adhering to the acid of the flame and precipitated by the nitrous salt of the spring water.

The remaining experiments of the **MASTER INVENTOR OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR**, revealed in the **triple kingdom of nature** <note>The animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms</note>, we leave to his own credit. I have brought forward only those matters which caused me doubt, and regarding which you desired my judgment. I hope that you will take these reflections in good part. With this, I commend you and your most honored family to divine protection, and myself to your friendly goodwill. Farewell.

->YOUR EXCELLENCY’S<-
->most devoted,<-
->**FREDERIC HOFFMANN, MD**<-

<meta>This page continues the "Judgment on the Gold of the Air," where the author evaluates a specific alchemical process. The previous page introduced the concept of the "magnet" (nitre) attracting a substance from the atmosphere. Here, the author details the physical manifestations of this "Gold of the Air" and then offers a skeptical chemical critique, suggesting the results may be due to the effects of the fire's heat rather than a true atmospheric extraction.</meta>

# JUDGMENT ON THE GOLD OF THE AIR

According to the **INVENTOR**, you will see that he has attracted from the **AIR** <note>original: *ex AURA*</note>, both internally and externally through magnetism, a grassy green color of the highest intensity. After it has cooled, it takes on a blue color, similar to that of the sky <note>original: *Eranii*; likely referring to a "Uranian" or celestial blue</note>. In the processing of this substance, these precautions must be observed: if you apply a slightly stronger fire to the "green lion" <note>a common alchemical term for a raw mineral or acidic state, often associated with vitriol or antimony</note> within the magnetism, it will return to its original chaos and leave the magnetism—or **nitre**—entirely empty, turning white again just as it was before. Therefore, opportunely and while it is still hot, pound the magnetism (which is still imbued with the green lion) in a mortar. If you then dissolve this in spring water, it will become green and a purple powder will be precipitated, which must then be washed. In the same way, if you pour **spirit of wine** <note>concentrated alcohol</note> over this magnetism once it has been reduced to powder and allow the mixture to digest, it will gently extract the "blood of the green lion" or the "golden sulfur" in a red form, for the preparation of the **POTABLE GOLD OF THE AIR**. If you wish to prepare **POTABLE GOLD** from this, use this precipitated golden sulfur, wash it, and having joined it with the Spirit of the World <note>original: *Spiritu mundi*; the vital life-force believed to permeate the universe</note>, fix it according to the art. Or, if you are ignorant of these methods, distill the spirit of wine that has been tinged with the **GOLD OF THE AIR** until only a fourth part remains; you will then have a residue of **Potable Gold** with a most pleasing reddish appearance. Although this initially shows some saltiness, after a few days the Magnetism settles like clear water while the tincture floats on top. This tincture should be shielded from the sun’s rays in a glass vessel, for if exposed to the sun, that **GOLD** quickly loses its color. These are the claims of the **LORD INVENTOR CONCERNING THE GOLD OF THE AIR**.

Truly, I would easily yield to the genius of our **INVENTOR**, provided his claims were animated by experimental certainty. However, his preparation of this "magnetism" leaves a doubt in my mind: was that golden sulfur (or **GOLD OF THE AIR**) truly attracted from the Air by the magnetism or the fixed nitre, or was it actually drawn from the flame of the fire, which contains acid within itself? For anyone who knows how the flame of a fire can often produce remarkable alterations in the arrangement of the parts of a body—changing its texture and the shape of its particles—will hold his judgment in suspense regarding this preparation. Even if no "golden sulfur" or "Gold of the Air" actually entered from the atmosphere, such changes can occur. For who, by this same reasoning, would not also mistake the **Tincture of Salt of Tartar** <note>potassium carbonate</note> for a genuine **Solar Tincture** <note>an alchemical essence of gold</note>, simply because it was extracted with spirit of wine from Salt of Tartar that had been calcined to a blue color by the heat of the fire? What is it that adorns steel, when heated white-hot in glowing coals, with such various colors so pleasing to the eye? What bestows such a great change of colors upon lead when it is exposed to the flames of Vulcan <note>the Roman god of fire; here meaning the furnace</note>?
<margin>See the aforementioned Robert Boyle in his treatise *On Colors*.</margin>
These matters are of such weight, that anyone...

<sig>Aa</sig>
<meta>catchword: attra-</meta>

<summary>This page details the "Inventor's" process for creating "Potable Gold" from atmospheric "magnetism" (nitre) before the author transitions into a skeptical critique. The author argues that the color changes observed (the "green lion" and purple precipitates) might be mere chemical reactions caused by the heat and acidity of the furnace fire rather than the successful capture of a celestial spirit, citing Robert Boyle's work on color as a reference.</summary>
<keywords>potable gold, green lion, nitre, magnetism, Robert Boyle, salt of tartar, alchemical skepticism, atmospheric spirit, calcination</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page concludes the skeptical assessment by Friedrich Hoffmann. While the previous page described the physical process of "attracting" a green and then blue substance from the air, Hoffmann here provides his chemical counter-argument. He suggests the results are not atmospheric gold, but rather the result of fire and humidity acting upon the minerals used in the experiment.</meta>

### RECENTLY PUBLISHED

aa 2

<warning>Significant bleed-through from the following page is visible, showing mirrored text from a title page.</warning>

Since the attraction of Gold from the **AIR** <note>original: *ex AURA*</note> was performed by means of magnetism, one might not undeservedly doubt and come to this opinion: that this Magnetism—or **nitre** <note>a chemical salt, likely potassium nitrate, used here as a reactive agent</note>—having been deprived of its native moisture by the flame of the fire, becomes extremely sharp. When this is afterwards entrusted to moist air (in which the **Salt of the air** is undoubtedly hidden), it undergoes various changes in its texture and its parts, both on the surface and internally. These are the causes of the different colors, as shown by the examples of salt of tartar, steel, and lead mentioned above. 

From this it happens that when **spirit of wine** <note>concentrated alcohol</note> is poured over the nitre impregnated with the sky-blue tincture <note>original: *tincturâ Eraneâ*</note>, this acid of the flame concentrated within it—due to the alteration and change of the texture and the shape of the parts—degenerates into the inventor's **Potable Gold**. As for the powder that settles at the bottom, I recognize it as earthly dross <note>waste material or impurities</note> adhering to the acid of the flame and precipitated by the nitrous salt of the spring water. The remaining experiments of the **LORD INVENTOR OF THE GOLD OF THE AIR**, revealed across the three kingdoms of nature <note>the traditional division of the natural world into animal, vegetable, and mineral</note>, we entrust to his own credit. I have only brought forward those which caused me doubt and regarding which you desired my judgment. I hope that you take these remarks in good part. With this, I commend you and your most honored family to divine protection, and myself to your friendly goodwill. Farewell.

->YOUR EXCELLENCY’S<-
->most devoted<-
->**FRIEDRICH HOFFMANN, MD**<-

The secrets of the nature of things, while its Initiates <note>original: *Mystae*—a term borrowing from ancient Greek mystery religions to describe dedicated scientific investigators</note> in this our century curiously investigate them, seem to come forth as truly divine things into the sight of mortals. And indeed, these things see the light and are made known—not without the nod of the Most High—so that His glory and eternal honor may rightly shine forth from them and be spread among us. For this is the ultimate end of secrets and of all things: an end which Satan—the most bitter enemy of both divine and human affairs—seems to attack once more in this utterly perverse century through war and the overturning of kingdoms, soon to change our most learned century into mere barbarism (unless God forbid it). But since nothing happens without the divine will, we must rest in it, and equally...

->To the Academy of the Nature-Curiosities of the Holy Roman Empire,<- <note>original: *S. R. Imp. Academiæ, Naturæ-Curiosorum*; this refers to the Leopoldina, the oldest continuously existing learned society in the world, founded in 1652.</note>

### TO THE MAGNIFICENT, MOST EXCELLENT
